run locally by run.py integration and inside the pre-merge / full / nightly profiles; historically a CI job (check Integration suite, own CI-only port block — Actions retired 2026-08-27); NOT a PR gate
⚠️ Reconciled with .github/workflows (2026-07-03): OWASP ZAP is not wired in any pipeline (previously shown as deployed). Since GitHub Actions was retired for this repo (Kam, 2026-08-27, KS-685) no workflow runs: the four platform suites (Schemathesis, Playwright, k6 smoke, Akto) plus touched unit suites are run locally by the PR author and recorded in the PR's Test Evidence block (Blockchain/Dev/docs/DEV-PROCESS.md §Manual CI-gate equivalents); the nightly/comprehensive counterparts (once folded into nightly-platform-suites.yml, with the pre-merge contract sweep in pre-merge-platform-suites.yml) are historical — those workflows cannot be dispatched and are not pending promotion; run the profiles locally.
APIFuzzer/Nuclei are not part of any current plan.
Historical — none of this runs today: GitHub Actions is retired for this repo (Kam, 2026-08-27, KS-685), so the workflows and required checks named here are dormant; the suites run locally (DEV-PROCESS.md §Manual CI-gate equivalents). 🔄 CI topology migrated (2026-07-06) — build-once → clone-across-isolated-stacks. The four PR suites then ran under ONE workflow, pr-platform-suites.yml (nightly: nightly-platform-suites.yml): a single reusable _build-images.yml job builds all 15 platform images fresh per run and pushes them to ghcr, then each suite job pulls those exact images and boots a fully-isolated stack (unique COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME/STACK_PREFIX + per-suite host-port offset, each tool pointed at its own gateway; those CI-only per-suite ports are historical and are not slot ports — local ports are the slot-1 base + 100·(N−1), e.g. gateway 6882 + 100·(N−1)).
Tool coverage/status below is unchanged; only the orchestration changed.
The per-suite files (pr-schemathesis/pr-playwright/performance-pr/akto-pr + the four nightly files) have been removed (folded into the two callers). Cutover: repoint branch-protection required checks to the new check-run display names (GitHub matches by display name, not job id): build, Schemathesis suite, Playwright suite, Performance suite (k6 smoke), Akto suite (PR) (renamed from Akto suite (PR-gate subset) — KS-413), plus the new pre-merge check Akto pre-merge (full).
Note: Schemathesis coverage below is read-path only (KS-428). The gateway's CSRF guard is inverted (it skips CSRF when no Authorization header is present and enforces it when one is), so every mutating request from a bearer-token client is rejected with 403 CSRF_TOKEN_MISSING (POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE alike), and 403 is an accepted status — so a sweep can report 0 failed across all 344 operations without having exercised any create, update or delete path.
The endpoint counts in the matrix are operations attempted, not write paths verified.
Treat a green Schemathesis run accordingly until KS-428 is resolved.

(every PR)Schemathesis 
# pr profile (local PR gate, run by the author; formerly pr-platform-suites.yml → schemathesis job, Actions retired 2026-08-27) — spec: docs/openapi/secuura-api.yaml · gateway: the named slot's, 6882 + 100·(N−1) (http://localhost:6882/api on slot 1)
# KS-191 (local): SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 python3 scripts/run.py setup (via scripts/setup.py) seeds LOCAL_LOGIN_LIMITER_DISABLED=true; also export it in your shell before `docker compose up` (Blockchain/Dev) so the gateway skips the per-IP login limiter (non-prod only) — else a full sweep's re-logins 429 and requests fall through unauthenticated.
# KS-955: run.py logs the sweep's default persona in ONCE before it sends anything (scripts/runner/persona_preflight.py). A failure STOPS the run with "KS-955 precondition:" and the causes in order — not seeded (ALLOW_DEFAULT_SEED_PASSWORDS / ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD in Blockchain/Dev/.env before the build), locked out, wrong password — instead of a 401 storm or an INVALID sweep.
SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 python3 scripts/run.py pr # KS-1386: the slot is required locally and the gateway is derived from it · wraps the schemathesis CLI call below with the pr profile's flags · persona auth is on by default (ENABLE_HOOKS=0 opts out)
# KS-1680 — ONE RUN IS ONE DRAW: three pr runs on one build found 11 / 9 / 8 failures. Every run prints "Seed: N (random | fixed by the profile | fixed via --seed)" — same words in schemathesis-results.json (summary.seed_label) and the HTML Overview.
SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 python3 scripts/run.py pr --repeat 3 # N=2-10 complete normal runs on distinct seeds (--seed S → S..S+N-1) + each failing operation swept ALONE on the same seeds (--no-isolate skips) → output/repeats/<set>/repeat-report.html: per pair k/N (N/N deterministic, k/N seed-dependent) + "needs other operations' data" when it never fails alone
SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 python3 scripts/run.py pr --repeat 3 --compare output/repeats/<earlier set> # before/after: "new" only 0/N → N/N; N/N → 0/N is "fixed" only on a schema/content-type/header check whose failing status the later set reached (else "not reached"), and "gone (not proven fixed)" on a check that fails on its status; anything else inconclusive · the set's baseline gate fails an untriaged pair seen in ANY run ("NEW … 1/3 seed-dependent")
schemathesis run http://localhost:6882/api/openapi.json \
  --checks=not_a_server_error,response_schema_conformance,status_code_conformance,content_type_conformance,response_headers_conformance \
  --mode=positive \
  --max-examples=5     # max-examples per profile: pr=5, test-fast=6, pre-merge=6, full=25, nightly=1100. MEASURED wall clock (KS-589, 2026-08-11, Ubuntu VM 8 vCPU — NOT this Mac): pr 1.2 min | pre-merge 16.9 min | full 26.5 min | nightly ~4 hrs (formula-derived, never measured end to end). KS-1408 re-measured full on this Mac (slot 4, 2026-10-05, 4.29.3): 17.4 min (sweep 11.8); KS-1439 (2026-10-07, slot 4, other sessions' stacks up on the machine): 19.6 min at 4.29.3, 20.9 and 20.3 min at 4.29.4 — not a speed comparison; pr/pre-merge/nightly NOT re-measured on this Mac — see the Measured timings table below. NOTE: wall clock is the coded pytest suites (~6.3 min floor, of which anchoring is 5.5 and is poll-bound, so it does not shrink on a faster host) PLUS the sweep — not max-examples alone
  --phases=examples,fuzzing   # pr/test-fast/local-debug/auth-rate-limited use 2 phases; pre-merge/full/nightly use all 4 (examples,coverage,fuzzing,stateful)
  --request-timeout=10 \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer $API_TOKEN"  # v4: --auth is basic only; use -H for Bearer
# KS-544 — the full applicable CLI surface is now DECLARED CONFIG, not defaulted by omission. Every run also carries:
  --continue-on-failure                          # POLICY, every profile, not overridable (run_options.CONTINUE_ON_FAILURE). Without it a scenario stops at its first failing case, so later cases never run and the report's blank Check Matrix cells read as "passed". A truncated run is not a result. --max-failures is therefore never set.
# NOT passed: --suppress-health-check. Suppression is ROOT-ONLY in schemathesis config, so one entry silences the check for all 344 operations — the blanket-masking pattern KS-156 argued against. Both former entries were removed in KS-544 (too_slow = a strategy-build timing signal we do not act on; filter_too_much = budget-sensitive per KS-500, so it means "this profile's budget is too low", not "this endpoint is broken"). The single endpoint that needed relief carries a per-operation generation.max-examples = 30 in schemathesis.toml instead. NB the old mechanism (a Hypothesis profile in the sweep subprocess) never took effect anyway — schemathesis overrides it.
  --generation-database=none      # NO cross-run example DB (KS-544; scripts/runner/args_builder.py). It was briefly persisted so a once-failing input would replay, but that benefit was never shown and it was MEASURED to cause a false failure (stored entries replayed, filtered out, tripped filter_too_much on POST /api/timestamps/batch). Passed explicitly so the report's Run Settings records the decision.
  --generation-codec=utf-8 --generation-allow-x00=true --generation-with-security-parameters=true  # match the library defaults, stated explicitly: allow-x00 keeps NUL generation on, which is what KS-472's control_byte_acceptance_guard exists to observe.
# NOT passed: --no-color. Coloured console output is kept (operator preference). The earlier rationale — that ANSI would corrupt the WARNINGS parse — was WRONG: parsing/warnings.py has stripped ANSI since KS-495, before any line matching. That strip is what makes colour safe; the HTML report takes its colour from report.css.
  --generation-maximize=response_time              # NIGHTLY ONLY — targeted PBT costs example budget the short profiles need for breadth.
# Defaults + a reason for each live in config/schemathesis_tests.yaml → harness.execution / harness.generation; flags deliberately NOT
#   adopted (--auth, --proxy, --tls-verify, --request-cert[-key], --header, --generation-graphql-allow-null, --force-color,
#   --exclude-checks, --warnings) are listed under harness.deliberately_unused_flags WITH a reason, so a missing flag is either
#   documented or a bug — tests/unit/runner/test_args_builder.py asserts both directions against the real yaml.
# NOT set: --max-redirects. It caps how many redirects are FOLLOWED, not whether they are, so 0 raises TooManyRedirects on the first
#   hop — a global engine error, not a check result. Redirects are always followed, so a 3xx op's checks see the target's response;
#   /api/m365/callback is excluded (KS-219) and the residual gap is tracked in KS-548.
# The run's EFFECTIVE flags are rendered in the report's Overview → Run Settings section, derived from the captured argv
#   (command-metadata.json) rather than from the yaml — so an override or a resolution bug is visible instead of hidden.
# KS-1408 — every run also writes all SEVEN report formats: --report junit,vcr,har,ndjson,json,wfc,allure. `wfc` (new in 4.29.0) goes to
#   schemathesis-output.wfc.json via --report-wfc-path → Overview "Status-code coverage (WFC)": HTTP statuses seen per endpoint,
#   endpoints that never answered 2xx, fault categories named (not bare codes), documented statuses never observed per endpoint, and WFC's fault count reconciled to the verdict by upstream's rule (unique failures − unmapped types − per-op repeats; never equality).
# KS-1408 — READ THE OUTCOME, NOT THE BARE EXIT CODE (4.29.0 changed them): 0 PASSED · 1 FAILED (findings to triage) ·
#   2 INVALID (nothing tested, or a schema/config/internal error — nothing to triage) · 124 TIMED OUT (harness wall clock) ·
#   130 INTERRUPTED (Ctrl+C) · other UNKNOWN. Console "Status:", results.json and the report's Verdict "Outcome" row agree.
# KS-1615 — each run also writes Schemathesis's native HTML report at <run>/schemathesis-native-html/index.html (linked from the harness report).
# KS-1607 — prove the method_not_allowed gate end to end: venv/bin/python3 -m pytest -q --no-cov tests/functional/config/test_method_not_allowed_gate_end_to_end.py
# KS-1674 — prove object_level_authorization finds a leak with [auth.wfc] peers and misses it without: venv/bin/python3 -m pytest -q --no-cov tests/functional/config/test_object_level_authorization_runs.py
# KS-1703 — the six GET-by-id leaks, two users, live (xfail markers off with --runxfail; slot N up, run through run-in-slot.sh N): venv/bin/python3 -m pytest -q --no-cov tests/test_get_by_id_object_authorization.py
# Full 12-check set (pre-merge/full/nightly only; config/schemathesis_tests.yaml harness.default_checks): not_a_server_error, response_schema_conformance, status_code_conformance,
#   content_type_conformance, response_headers_conformance, negative_data_rejection, positive_data_acceptance,
#   use_after_free, ensure_resource_availability, unsupported_method, missing_required_header, allow_header_conformance (KS-684, new in 4.25.0).
#   The built-in `ignored_auth` is NOT in the list (KS-1271): the custom `ignored_auth_rate_limit_aware` replaces it — it calls the built-in but scores a 429 on the no-auth probe as INCONCLUSIVE, not as an auth gap.
#   (unsupported_method and missing_required_header ride the coverage phase at zero added request cost — KS-226's harness.warnings.fail_on: [method_not_allowed] also gates these.)
# KS-406 note: the coverage-phase unexpected-method PROBES are disabled ([phases.coverage] unexpected-methods = []) —
#   authenticated routing answers undeclared verbs auth-first (a clean sweep saw 405+Allow on 619/652), so unsupported_method
#   no longer emits sweep findings. That one signal is instead covered by KS-416's pytest guardrail
#   (tests/test_undeclared_method_safety.py): authenticated undeclared verbs must be safely rejected (401/403/404/405),
#   never execute (2xx) or crash (5xx); 3 known platform exceptions are Known-Failure-tracked (recorded as a pytest xfail).
# KS-416 CI promotion: the FULL live integration pytest suite (pytest tests/, incl. this guardrail) runs locally via
#   run.py quality (needs a NAMED SLOT — only `run.py quality:static` is offline, KS-1386) AND gates PRE-MERGE + NIGHTLY (run.py pre-merge/nightly, via _run_pytest_integration) — NOT the PR gate.
#   Runs under the real 2000/60s rate limit (not disabled); the KS-416 test retries/skips on 429. Verified 282 pass / 0 429.
# KS-507 de-flake: the undeclared-verb 429 retry is now bounded exponential backoff honouring Retry-After within a wall-clock
#   budget (skip only on a genuine deadline breach); the anchor/export workflow polls are deadline-bounded (ANCHOR_SETTLE_TIMEOUT /
#   EXPORT_SETTLE_TIMEOUT) and record the observed settle time, surfaced in the report's Workflow Timings tab (present only when a
#   settle-recording integration run produced pytest-integration-output.xml; a normal sweep's report is unchanged).
# KS-478 (KS-466 §11) coded regression: tests/test_state_machine_preconditions.py drives the 10 state-machine/precondition ops
#   (governance submit/cancel/execute/vote, transfers reject, governance delegate, referrals apply, stake unstake, m365 sites/documents-sync)
#   to their UNMET precondition and pins each refusal to a clean enveloped business 4xx (never a raw 5xx / silent 2xx) — so the sweep's
#   "rejected schema-compliant request" reports on these ops are proven false positives (contract-side fix = stateful links, KS-188). Runs
#   standalone (_run_pytest_preconditions → pytest-preconditions-output.xml), gates pre-merge/full/nightly, surfaced in a dedicated Preconditions report tab.
#   A skip is NOT a pass (KS-478 red→green rule): an env-unsatisfiable/unseeded vector skips → suite drops to INCOMPLETE (amber) and FAILS the gate; console/report show "N of 10 ops exercised". Fix = provision the persona/provider, or explicit pytest.xfail.
# KS-1762 OPT-IN, DESTRUCTIVE: tests/governance_cap_live proves the KS-1719 cap free live — a cap of active + queued proposals from two proposers
#   (the free cancels only the super-admin's 3 oldest) and a cap it cannot touch (skip reason says "freed 0"). Never collected without
#   SECUURA_RUN_GOVERNANCE_CAP_LIVE=1, never run by a sweep or the quality gate. Needs a slot with no active/queued proposals and no other DRep.
#   run.py governance-cap provisions the generated actors first, runs the suite alone and renders its own HTML report tab (Governance cap) — console = JUnit = report.
systemTest/run-in-slot.sh 1 python3 scripts/run.py governance-cap
# Custom checks (config/checks.py, self-register when persona hooks are on = default). KS-1170: they ARE part of the --checks set —
#   declared once in schemathesis_tests.yaml harness.custom_checks and appended by the runner to every profile/category list, because
#   Schemathesis >= 4.26.1 disables any custom check an explicit --checks list omits (the then-five were silently OFF from the 4.27.1 bump, 2026-09-14,
#   until this landed). SIX are declared today (harness.custom_checks): integration_5xx_guard, by_design_501_guard, by_design_input_tolerance_guard, control_byte_acceptance_guard, public_operation_auth_conformance, ignored_auth_rate_limit_aware (KS-1271).
# KS-1447 — GET /api/users/admin/list is the 5th by_design_input_tolerance_guard operation: negative_data_rejection accepts 2xx there, and the guard fails everything except an undeclared query key (Kam, KS-592, 2026-08-26, "unknown-query-param class"). Strict classifier config/unknown_query_tolerance.py; declared limit/offset violations still fail (baselined KS-1015); offset=-1 HTTP 500s still fail not_a_server_error (KS-593).
#   Pinned yaml <-> registry, per-profile argv, and live-CLI (tests/functional/config/test_not_a_server_error_suppression.py).
#   integration_5xx_guard (KS-268 + KS-499 + KS-544) restores genuine-500 coverage on 14 by-design-503 integration endpoints where
#     not_a_server_error tolerates ONLY the declared 503 (per-op expected-statuses, KS-1170 — was enabled=false, dead on 4.26.1+): 3 GET reads
#     (billing payment-methods, onedrive files, teams webhook-config — KS-268) +
#     7 write ops (billing customers create/update/setup-intent/default-payment-method, payment-methods delete, POST /credentials,
#     POST /anchors/thread-mint — KS-499, unmasked post-KS-428 CSRF fix). Passes the declared 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE envelope, fails any other 5xx.
#   by_design_501_guard (KS-686) — the same treatment for the ONE operation that answers a deliberate 501: POST /api/wallets/verify fails
#     closed (KS-488 C-2) until KS-627 lands. not_a_server_error tolerates only the 501 for it in schemathesis.toml (KS-1170); the guard passes ONLY the declared
#     501 NOT_IMPLEMENTED envelope and fails a raw 500 / 502 / non-envelope 501. Ruling verbatim beside _BY_DESIGN_501_OPS (Kam, 2026-08-26).
#     Expiry tripwire = tests/test_wallet_verify_stub.py (RED naming KS-627 when the 501 posture ends → delete both halves).
#   public_operation_auth_conformance (KS-425) fails a declared-public (security: []) SAFE READ (GET/HEAD) that answers
#     401/403 to an unauthenticated request — otherwise persona auth sends it no token and its authed contract silently
#     goes untested. Read-scoped so credential endpoints (POST /api/auth/login is security:[] yet 401s a bad password) aren't false-flagged.
#     (ignored_auth catches only the opposite: bearer-marked but open). Per-tool spec-consumption audit + decision:
#     systemTest/docs/how-to-test-secuura.md → Spec Consumption by Tool.
# KS-492 (Review G) security regressions — 6 integration tests/, run in pre-merge/nightly.
# KS-571 (2026-10-06) — CONNECTOR-ONLY onBehalfOf: config/connector_only_field.py (examples-phase seam: map_case + before_add_examples) drops `onBehalfOf` from POSITIVE cases on the 6 honouring operations (POST /api/documents, .../{id}/version, .../share, .../transfer-custody, .../revoke, POST /api/certifications/issue), because a JWT persona may not send it and the platform answers 400 "onBehalfOf is connector-only". The toml's old widened positive_data_acceptance statuses that included 400 for /share and /transfer-custody are REMOVED (they masked every other 400); the refusal itself is pinned by tests/test_connector_only_refusal.py, and tests/unit/hooks/test_connector_only_field.py holds the set equal to the published spec.
# KS-571 live tests added 2026-10-06: test_connector_only_refusal · test_unhandled_rejection_posture · test_status_list_authz · test_connector_identity · test_connector_attribution · test_connector_key_lifecycle (connector-key fixture, KS-568); integration/: test_seed_demo_gate · test_billing_webhook_idempotency · test_connector_provenance_off_chain.
# KS-1414 — known 4.29.x false-positive class, kept VISIBLE (never filtered/baselined): a negative case mutates only the body, then a pool-drawn value overwrites the mutation, so a VALID body is sent, the platform rightly accepts it, and negative_data_rejection still says "Invalid data should have been rejected" (55–95 per full sweep). A dedicated report section + console block + results.json block COUNT them (body-only-negative AND the sent body validates against the spec); a body-only case that sent no body is KS-1364's class, counted separately. Informational, not a gate. Since 2026-10-07 the by-design guard (by_design_input_tolerance_guard) shares the rule: a request valid as sent no longer fails it (6 of its 12 failures on the KS-1439 sweeps; the other 6, audit-log limit=201, are genuine) and the section counts the guard's failures and valid-as-sent passes.
# Runner banner (KS-571, reverses KS-684's narrower banner): the closing banner now lists EVERY post-run gate (run validity, NDJSON completeness, report reconciliation, triaged baseline) and the gated suites (IDOR, integration, anchoring, preconditions, auth coverage), and names a SKIPPED gated suite with its reason, so the banner and the exit code cannot disagree (it used to print OVERALL: PASSED over exit 1).
# Legend: a Known Failure (recorded as a pytest xfail) = a test asserting the desired behaviour the platform does not do
#   yet (a documented, tolerated gap); an Unexpected Pass (a pytest xpass) = such a test that unexpectedly passed → flip it
#   to a hard assertion. Unfixed findings are a Known Failure (xfail(strict=True) — flips to a hard failure the moment the fix lands)/skip so they document the vuln without
#   red-ing the gate, and auto-flip when the platform fix lands:
#   OUT OF SCOPE (KS-519) = the fifth outcome, and NOT a skip: a scenario this TARGET cannot contain, as opposed to a vector
#   this environment could have driven and did not. Worked example: test_revoked_document_does_not_verify asserts on a real
#   on-chain anchor, and local anchoring is mock BY DESIGN since KS-535, so it had hard-failed every local run 13 times
#   without executing once. It is excluded from the gate and printed with its reason — failing on it would red every local
#   run, and a permanently-red gate is not consulted, which loses the same signal a silent pass would. Recognised ONLY by
#   the OUT_OF_SCOPE_SKIP_PREFIX constant (so an ordinary pytest.skip still reds), and a run that exercised NOTHING fails
#   regardless, so the exclusion can never empty a suite quietly. Not an xfail: an xfail asserts an open PLATFORM defect.
#   A (KS-486): test_notification_bola.py — non-admin creating a notification for another userId must 403 (handler returns
#     201 today → Known Failure).
#   B (KS-487): test_webhook_ssrf.py — (a) guard pins originate's existing SSRF literal/scheme protection on POST /api/webhooks
#     (passing); (b) DNS-rebind: a hostname resolving to an internal IP (redis/postgres docker svc, 169.254.169.254.nip.io) is

# output: each run writes its own timestamped folder reports/performance-reports/<scenario>-<UTC-ts>/ (summary.json · report.html · gate-report.json · run-metadata.json · memory.json when obs is up); latest symlink → newest run; gate resolves the newest run automatically (gate/cli.ts --scenario <name>)
# summary: deprecated --summary-export replaced by a k6 handleSummary() hook (helpers/summary.ts) writing the same schema the gate reads; target path via PERF_SUMMARY_PATH. k6's web-dashboard HTML is unchanged and remains the only HTML report.
# memory-leak monitoring (advisory): after a run, when observability is up, pulls per-container memory (cAdvisor container_memory_usage_bytes{id!=""}) from Prometheus (PROMETHEUS_URL, default http://localhost:9090), computes a growth slope, writes memory.json + pushes a Grafana gauge. Container names are resolved cross-platform from the docker ps id→name table (include/exclude via PERF_MEMORY_INCLUDE/PERF_MEMORY_EXCLUDE), so it works on Docker Desktop for Mac (no cAdvisor name label) as well as Linux (AWS EC2 / Azure VM). VERDICT only for endurance scenarios (soak/nightly/soak-short); shorter runs collect but are "not assessed". Never changes the exit code; silent no-op when obs is down.
# thresholds: http_req_duration: p(95)<500,p(99)<1500 · http_req_failed: rate<0.01 · checks: rate>0.99
# v2 removals: --no-summary→--summary-mode=disabled · k6 cloud run script.js · externally-controlled executor gone
# v2.3 (KS-1354): every run passes --features freeze-env (a script write to __ENV throws instead of leaking into later iterations; native-histograms stays opt-in via PERF_NATIVE_HISTOGRAMS) · the console banner, run-metadata.json k6Image and the HTML report banner name the image · pin = config/observability.yml, Dockerfile FROM must match (tests/unit/config/k6ImagePinParity.test.ts) · new --scenario / --once not used: every script runs k6's single default scenario · every series carries K6_PROMETHEUS_RW_LABELS perf_scenario + k6_image (filter Grafana on perf_scenario — k6's own scenario tag is always "default") · --log-ns-timestamps in the -k6.log (runner now needs k6 ≥ 2.3.0) · an unknown --features name → INVALID run, exit 3 · a counter rate k6 left unset reads n/a, not 0.00/s
# all scenarios (config/scenarios.yml): smoke · load · stress · spike · soak · nightly · scalability · auth-spike — each has a non-blocking -short dev variant (never use -short as a CI gate/baseline)

🧱 Stack slots — four configurations at once (KS-666)
ItemDetail
No slot named?Refused, LOCAL only (KS-1016, 2026-09-11). Every harness — Schemathesis, k6, Playwright, Akto and the shared provisioning step — stops at start when the target is local (localhost, 127.0.0.1, [::1], host.docker.internal) and neither SECUURA_STACK_SLOT nor the platform-s alias STACK_SLOT is set, and prints the command to run instead: 
No stack slot named. Every LOCAL system-test run must say which Docker stack it targets
(target: http://localhost:6882/api). Name the slot and run again, for example:
  SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=<n> python3 scripts/run.py pr
  or: systemTest/run-in-slot.sh <n> python3 scripts/run.py pr
Remote targets need no slot; CI (CI=true) is exempt.
Remote targets (demo, a tunnel) carry no slot and are left alone; CI is exempt because its isolated per-suite stacks are local and deliberately unslotted (CI=true — a convention, not a lock).
Before this rule an unset slot meant slot 1 silently.
Pick a slotSECUURA_STACK_SLOT=N ./Start_Up/start-secuura.sh.
Four slots; every published port is the historic one plus 100 × (N − 1).
Gateway 6882 + 100 × (N − 1) (slot 1: 6882).
Pair with platform-sThe numbering is shared (PS-622): S slot N and K slot N line up with no configuration. platform-s exports STACK_SLOT and platform-k falls back to it, so one export drives both.
Point the suitessource systemTest/slot-target.sh — Schemathesis, Playwright, Akto (with its own side-stack, mongo volume and dashboard port) and the performance runner all follow.
Akto and the performance runner used to default to localhost:6882 alone, so a run silently tested slot 1; since KS-1386 an unslotted local run is refused instead — there is no default URL. Playwright is the exception since KS-682: it reads SECUURA_STACK_SLOT itself, derives the gateway (6882 + 100·(N−1)), refuses a malformed or out-of-range slot, and refuses a label naming a different slot — unsourced SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=<n> npx playwright test targets slot <n> or fails saying why, never slot 1 in silence (KS-1056 tracks the other three).
Fresh local stack: the bootstrap adminEvery harness provisions its actors through fixtures/provision-actors.ts, which grants roles as admin@secuura.com using BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD.
That account exists only if the auth seeder received ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD at build time — set it in Blockchain/Dev/.env (line 133 of the template) before start-secuura.sh --rebuild, and export the same value as BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD in the shell that runs the suite.
Until 2026-09-10 the value was never forwarded to the container, so it did nothing and every fresh stack 401'd (KS-972); compose now forwards it (KS-682, from KS-971) and check-stack-safety.sh refuses a compose file that drops it.
If provisioning prints DEGRADED, this is the first thing to check.
Since 2026-09-10 Playwright's global-setup hands provisioning its own slot-derived gateway (--base-url), so an unsourced SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=N run provisions on slot N — before that it provisioned against slot 1 whatever slot the suite ran on, DEGRADED when slot 1 was down, and coincided silently when it was up (KS-682).
The manifest, its rejected copy and any superseded copy all carry the slot segment (actors-slot<n>.superseded-<ts>.json), so a quarantined manifest keeps its attribution.
Schemathesis self-resolvesKS-1016: Schemathesis no longer depends on that export.
It reads SECUURA_STACK_SLOT (or STACK_SLOT) itself and derives the gateway by the same historic + 100 × (N − 1) rule, so SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=<n> alone targets :6882 + 100·(n−1).
One resolver answers for the runner, the hooks and the persona logins — they used to disagree, and a shell carrying only API_BASE_URL sent the runner to one slot and every login to another, inside a single run.
Edge cases (padding, trailing slashes, a blank-but-present variable, a whitespace SECUURA_STACK_SLOT masking STACK_SLOT) are pinned in tests/unit/test_slot_resolver_edges.py; the fixture, the compose project probe, the shell-executed slot rule and the best-effort pushes each have their own module, and the no-hardcoded-slot guard scans src/, scripts/ and config/ too.
Refuses what the shell refuses (0, 01, abc, 5).
Proven by rotation on slots 4, 3, 2 and 1, one at a time with the others torn down and every docker call audited (2026-09-10/11).
API Explorer names its slot (KS-1386)Generate with the slot: SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=N npm run generate -- ../../Blockchain/Dev/docs/openapi/secuura-api.yaml output/.
The explorer's Local server, its two health-check curls and its troubleshooting hints point at slot N's gateway (historic + 100 × (N − 1)); a local run that names no slot is refused — under CI too, since the explorer has no URL of its own.
Until 2026-09-29 every explorer pointed at slot 1, and because every explorer opened from disk shares one browser-storage origin, a server chosen on one slot's page was reused by another's — the page now ignores a stored server it does not offer. src/config/stackSlot.ts is the package's copy of the shared slot rule, pinned to fixtures/slot.ts by a parity test.
No slot named → refused, never slot 1 (KS-1386)Every harness refuses a LOCAL run that names no slot (SECUURA_STACK_SLOT or STACK_SLOT), at every step that touches a stack. CI=true/1/yes and remote targets need no slot, but with no slot and no URL the run is refused — there is no default URL.
An exempt run is labelled unslotted (metrics, run metadata, actors-unslotted.json).
Name the slot: SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=<n> npm run … or systemTest/run-in-slot.sh <n> ….
Akto's and Performance's refusals name the npm script you ran, so the command they show can be pasted back with the slot, e.g. SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 npm run test:pr.
BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD on EVERY runExport it in the shell of every suite run — Schemathesis's run.py provisions actors too, not only the TypeScript suites.
The FIRST provisioning on a fresh stack creates the five generated actors; without the variable they are created as OWNER (log: bootstrap admin: UNAVAILABLE, + … role=OWNER) and every later run finds them pre-existing, cannot re-role them, reports DEGRADED and — for Playwright — fails auth-setup with a 400 (KS-966: the admin password comes from the manifest).
A retry does not fix it: reset that slot's DB (start-secuura.sh --fresh-db) or use a new --seed, then rerun the series with the variable set.
Measured 2026-09-11 on slot 4 (KS-1016). Since KS-1016 provisioning refuses when the bootstrap admin is unavailable — nothing is created, the pre-suite says DEGRADED and why, and the next run with the variable set provisions correctly.
DEGRADED names what happened to the manifest (KS-981)When provisioning fails, any manifest on disk is moved aside (actors-slotN.superseded-<ts>.json) and the DEGRADED banner says which of three things happened: moved to <path>, none on disk, or could NOT be moved — the last naming the file to check (fixtures/generated/actors-slotN.json).
Since 2026-09-29 the strict arm (SYSTEMTEST_REQUIRE_PROVISIONED=true) says it too; before, it printed only FATAL, so a manifest left on disk for the next run went unreported, and the could-not-move advice named the old actors.json that nothing reads.
Push refused: NOTHING CHECKED (KS-1063)The pre-push hook always runs the systemTest/* format gate over the packages a push touches — for a systemTest-only push it is the only gate that runs.
A package with no deps installed is skipped and named.
Since 2026-09-29, if every selected package is skipped the push is refused with NOTHING CHECKED … This is NOT a formatting failure: run npm ci in each package it names and push again.
Before, it passed on 0 package(s) checked, N skipped, 0 failed — indistinguishable from a clean check.
Login failure says WHICH cause (KS-1167)KS-1167 (2026-09-14): the shared pre-suite login used to drop the HTTP status, so bootstrap admin: UNAVAILABLE explained every non-200 as “check the password” — including a 429 from the per-IP login limiter, which had never looked at the password.
It now names the cause: unreachable (with ECONNREFUSED <host>:<port> — was the bare fetch failed, the port lost in .cause), limiter (429: password NOT checked, wait the 15-minute window), rejected (401/400/403: check ADMIN_USER_PASSWORD / lockout), unexpected (5xx or a token-less 200: gateway health).
Playwright's auth-setup does the same for its own login (helpers/login-diagnosis.ts: limiter / EMPTY password from a withheld manifest / rejected / unhealthy, each naming its next step), and its global-setup now refuses on a 429 ping instead of proceeding into provisioning inside a cooldown.
Pinned by __tests__/bootstrap_login_diagnosis.test.sh (a stub gateway answering one fixed status, plus a port proven closed — no stack), performance/tests/unit/fixtures/{loginOutcome,describeError}.test.ts and the Playwright login-diagnosis / preflight-verdict unit files.
Slot gate driven as a PROCESS; unit suites slot-independent (KS-1167)Playwright and k6 proved their KS-1016 guard was called by reading the runner as text; Akto and Schemathesis already spawned theirs.
Both now spawn the real entry point too — and npm run report (Playwright), which resolved slot 1 silently, consults the rule (SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=1 npm run report).
Separately, the offline unit suites were not slot-independent: measured 2026-09-14, Playwright 348/348 unslotted but 345–347/348 from a slotted shell, Performance 1077/1077 vs 1060–1067 — files cleared the slot family only in afterEach, never cleared the STACK_SLOT alias, and k6's runner modules freeze their slot at import.
Fixed (Playwright: clear before each + a childEnv() strip for spawns; Performance: a vitest setupFiles strip before every file loads) and pinned by a matrix that re-runs the suite under no slot and slots 1–4 (both variables, and the wrapper's suffix shape) and demands identical counts.
STACK_SLOT alias everywhereEvery slot reader (shared manifest writer, Playwright, k6, Akto, Schemathesis) honours platform-s's STACK_SLOT in stack_env.sh:37's order: SECUURA_STACK_SLOT wins, a blank one falls through, a malformed alias is refused.
A STACK_SLOT=N-only shell used to drive slot N's gateway while opening slot 1's actors (KS-1016, 2026-09-11).
⚠ Cross-slot reads refusedKS-1016: the DB sweeps and the compose controller now refuse a stack slot other than the one the HTTP assertions target, naming both sides.
Previously docker exec secuura-postgres was hardcoded, so a slot-3 run counted rows in slot 1's database — and every slot shares one Postgres password (KS-731), so the wrong database answered rather than refusing.
The compose path was worse: it stops and recreates, so it took another ticket's stack down.
Run metrics per slotKS-1016: the Pushgateway grouping key carries the slot (/job/integration/environment/local/slot/<n>).
There is one shared secuura-obs-pushgateway on :9099, and a same-key push replaces the group — so two slots used to overwrite each other's run summary and the survivor silently described whichever pushed last.
Find your resultsFolders and files carry the Linear ticket and the slot, with no exception — results-ks-666-slot<n>.json, output/reports-slot<n>/pr-ks-666-<ts>, test-fast-ks-666-<ts>-slot<n>, playwright/results/playwright-report-ks-682-<ts>-slot<n> behind a stable results/latest-slot<n> pointer, with the report server on 9324 + 100·(N−1) so two slots' reports open side by side (KS-682).
Slot 1 is labelled too, so every result relates to a slot and therefore a port.
k6 dashboard portThe performance harness binds ONE host port itself — k6's live dashboard, 5665 + 100 × (N − 1) (slot 1: 5665) (KS-971).
It is not published by the stack; docker run -p maps it, and --no-obs takes no host port at all.
Registered in port-allocations.json as K6_WEB_DASHBOARD_PORT — it had been bound and offset per slot since KS-666 but recorded nowhere, so the crossover gate could not see it.
Pick a slot in GrafanaONE set of dashboards, never a copy per slot.
Every dashboard has a Stack slot picker top-left (slot1…slot4) — you pick a slot, never a port.
"All" is deliberately absent: several panels aggregate with a bare max()/sum(), so an All selection would silently merge four stacks into one number (KS-971).
⚠ App metrics: none yetThe secuura-services Prometheus job returns no series on any slot — no service implements /metrics (prom-client is a dependency of none; measured 2026-09-07, control /health).
Its ports were also all wrong (3000–3006 against the real 4000-range / 8080) until KS-971; prometheus_targets.test.sh now fails if they drift again.
The secuura-gateway job was removed: it targeted the gateway's published HOST port on /metrics while nginx serves /stub_status, which nginx-exporter already scrapes on 9113.
So platform CPU and memory come from cAdvisor (per-slot), not from the services themselves.
⚠ Concurrent ≠ comparableFour k6 runs may now execute at once safely — results, logs, metrics and log streams are all per-slot.
They still share the Docker Desktop allocation (8 CPUs / 31.3 GiB, docker info 2026-10-06), so a latency figure from a concurrent run is not a baseline.
Each run records machineShared in run-metadata.json — and on the gate report beside the verdict — so a contended run cannot later be quoted as a clean one (KS-971 narrows KS-435/KS-610; it does not repeal them).
Grafana → slot pickerPick slot1…slot4 at the top of any dashboard — never a port, never a per-slot copy.
Container panels take their name and slot from secuura_container_name_info, pushed per slot by the performance harness, because cAdvisor emits no name label on Docker Desktop (measured: 141 per-container ids, zero named).
Filter $slot on that join’s secuura_container_name_info side only — filtering the cAdvisor side as well is what left slots 2–4 rendering empty panels (KS-971).
Provision per slotsystemTest/run-in-slot.sh <N> npx tsx systemTest/fixtures/provision-actors.ts — each slot writes its own fixtures/generated/actors-slot<N>.json.
One shared actors.json was credential-safe but identity-unsafe: seed-derived emails and passwords are identical on every slot, but userId is issued by the stack, so an ownership assertion read another slot’s id (KS-971).
Prove it, don’t assume itVICTIM_SLOT=1 systemTest/performance/scripts/prove-slot-isolation.sh pauses the named victim slot’s service (VICTIM_SLOT is required since KS-1386; it used to default to a fixed slot), runs the same scenario on all four concurrently, and asserts the damage stayed inside that slot.
Four green runs prove nothing — they are equally consistent with all four harnesses testing slot 1 (KS-971).
⚠ CI ports differHistorically (CI, retired 2026-08-27) the four port blocks were assigned per suite because each job booted its own single-tenant stack — CI-only numbers, not slot ports.
Locally they are per slot: every value is the slot-1 base + 100·(N−1), derived in one place (Blockchain/Dev/scripts/stack_env.sh), never typed. One bounded exemption (KS-1450): the Schemathesis baseline's $generated.from_runs run ids record which sweep produced a row and cannot carry an inline marker, so a lone string element of that exact shape is exempt in that one file — and a guard cell asserts every exempted line really is a from_runs member, so the exemption cannot widen silently.
Reading one as the other attributes a result to the wrong thing.
⚠ One suite per slotSlots run in parallel; suites within a slot do not.
Use systemTest/run-in-slot.sh <slot> <command>.
The reason is the per-IP login rate limiter — every harness authenticates through the same gateway and every local suite shares one IP.
Three suites on slot 1 (2026-08-20) gave Schemathesis 300 engine errors / 289 operations, all login for persona=super_admin is rate-limited, while the report still read PASSED because nothing was exercised. All four harnesses are affected, not just Schemathesis — the others show it as login failures, empty results or timeouts. Once it appears, wait 15 minutes before starting any further tests.
What fitsMeasured on this Mac 2026-08-20: four platform-k stacks (33 containers each, 132 total) plus a platform-s stack in 7.2 GiB of a 31.3 GiB Docker Desktop allocation (recorded as 6 CPU on 2026-08-20; docker info now reports 8 CPU / 31.3 GiB, 2026-10-06 — the CPU count was not re-measured under load). Memory is not the constraint; CPU is — load ~23 with four stacks idle.
Four concurrent suites is unmeasured.
Akto refuses a cross-slot run (KS-687)Sourcing slot-target.sh is no longer the only thing between you and a run that scans one slot and reports as another.
The harness now refuses, at validate(), any run whose pieces name different slots (SECUURA_STACK_SLOT, AKTO_PREFIX, dashboard URL, scan target, artefact suffix — src/config/slot.ts), an unusable SECUURA_STACK_SLOT (5, abc, 3.5 — never defaulted to slot 1), an artefact suffix that is a path, and a COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME that would aim npm run start / db:ui at another project.
Pre-flight probes the same origin the scan is sent to (it used to probe a neighbour: the first live slot-4 run passed pre-flight and then filed 8,816 connectivity skips under a PASS).
After every scan the persisted artefact is walked and any local URL on another slot's port fails the run (src/scan/slotLeakCheck.ts).
Proven live on all four slots, 2026-09-10 (8,902–8,933 executed, 0 vulnerable, 2,619–2,635 references to the slot's own gateway, zero to any other), and again on slot 4 from the develop-merged tree 2026-09-11 (9,105 / 9,138 executed, zero foreign).
The gate that could not see it (KS-687 F2)Blockchain/Dev/scripts/check-akto-container-names.sh (6f2 of check-stack-safety.sh) scans src/ bin/ scripts/, *.ts *.sh *.mjs, for a slot-1 container name in any shape — a literal or a defaulted variable (${VAR:-akto-mongo}, ?? 'akto-testing').
Its non-vacuity assert names the directories the corpus must contribute and fails when one is absent — a file count passed over the directory F1 lived in.
17 cases, F1's original line first.
🔀 When to combine tools
ItemDetail
Schemathesis + AktoContract coverage + deep auth bypass & IDOR detection.
Run SC first; feed failing endpoints into Akto.
Schemathesis + k6Functional coverage validation (SC) then load/stress at scale (k6).
Prove correctness before k6 hammers it.
Semgrep + TrivySAST (source) + container/IaC scanning.
Semgrep finds code flaws; Trivy finds dependency & infra vulns.
Gitleaks + SemgrepPre-commit (gitleaks) → CI SAST (semgrep).
Prevent secrets before review; deep dataflow analysis in CI.
⚠️ Common pitfalls
PitfallFix
Using Schemathesis as a load tool.It sends sequential requests, not concurrent VUs. Use k6 for throughput & latency.
Relying on Akto for schema validation.Akto has no OpenAPI contract awareness. Schemathesis owns conformance.
k6 v2: keeping removed flags.--no-summary → --summary-mode=disabled; k6 cloud script.js → k6 cloud run; REST API needs --address.
Running --max-examples uncapped in CI.Will time out. Use pr=5 (1.2 min, KS-589 VM 2026-08-11), test-fast=6 (1,860 req by the request model; not re-measured), pre-merge=6 (16.9 min, KS-589 VM 2026-08-11; not re-measured on this Mac) for the local PR/pre-merge runs; full=25 (17.4 min measured on this Mac at 4.29.3, KS-1408, 2026-10-05; 19.6–20.9 min on 2026-10-07 with other sessions' stacks up, KS-1439; 26.5 min on the KS-589 VM) for on-demand comprehensive; nightly=1100 (~4 hrs) only as a deliberate local run.py nightly (the scheduled workflow is historical).

Codebase analysis (Distributed_Secuura, excl. systemTest): API is REST-only, OpenAPI 3.1 (docs/openapi/secuura-api.yaml, generated from Zod).
The standard we cite and target is OpenAPI 3.2.0 (released Sept 2025, a non-breaking superset); the published document stays 3.1.0 and every change must remain 3.1-valid until Akto is tested against 3.2 — its swagger-parser rejects a 3.2 document outright (KS-429).
Schemathesis already supports 3.2; Playwright and k6 do not read the spec at all. — 310 paths / 344 operations (parsed from secuura-api.yaml, 2026-10-06), single bearerAuth JWT scheme.
No GraphQL or gRPC surface (only the midnight service consumes an external GraphQL indexer).
27 Node/TS services + React/M365 frontends → Semgrep + ESLint cover SAST/lint; Aiken contracts are a SAST gap.
IaC = Azure Bicep + Docker Compose (prod runs on Azure Container Apps, not Kubernetes — k8s/swarm manifests are legacy). 🔑 Auth surface — connector tokens on the users routes (KS-564, KS-1402)
🔑 KS-1402
connector
on /lookupauth vitest 
# Kam ruling 2026-10-02 option (a): widen GET /api/users/lookup to accept a connector token, as POST /api/users/stub already does (KS-564, Option A).
# WHY: Platform S authenticates with an sk_ connector key, so its token is type:'connector'. /lookup used plain authenticate(), so every S transfer-custody call 401'd and fell back. Both routes now use authenticateAccessOrConnector().
# THE TOKEN TYPE IS NOT THE PERMISSION — the route's own role/scope gate still runs after it, so `users:read` is the effective gate on /lookup, and a cross-tenant lookup still returns 404 (not 200-empty: positive existence must not be readable from the response shape).
# AUDIT LABEL is route-neutral on purpose: the log line read "users/stub: ..." unconditionally, so a second accepting route would have written an audit line naming a route it never touched. It now carries route: req.baseUrl + req.path.
#   MEASURED on GET /api/users/lookup?email=alice%40example.com — req.originalUrl = /api/users/lookup?email=alice%40example.com (leaks the address); req.baseUrl + req.path = /api/users/lookup (does not). originalUrl would defeat maskEmail(); that is why it is not used here.
cd Blockchain/Dev/services/auth

RFC 3161 Timestamp Verification — KS-1404

What to know.POST /api/timestamps/verify used to accept an unsigned token: any DER blob containing the document digest read as valid, and a JSON {"hash":"…"} token read as valid too.
Both paths are closed.
Verification now checks the CMS signature over TSTInfo, the signed messageDigest, EKU id-kp-timeStamping, validity at genTime, and a chain to a configured trust anchor — and reads the imprint from the parsed TSTInfo rather than searching the tree.

Chain order is load-bearing pkijs takes the LAST element of certs as the end-entity (certs: [rootCa, intermediateCa, leafCert]). [signer, ...intermediates] validates an intermediate as the leaf and ACCEPTS a signer that chains to nothing. Correct form: [...intermediates, signer]

MFA disable NULLs the seed and the backup codes — KS-938

What to know. “MFA disabled” used to answer 200 while leaving the TOTP seed and the hashed backup codes in the row — and the seed could then be re-armed without a new one being minted. updateUser skips any field whose value is undefined, so all three clearing sites emitted a partial UPDATE.
They now pass null, which writes SQL NULL. The undefined skip itself is unchanged — other callers rely on it to mean “leave this column alone”.
One site (POST /me/mfa/disable) did not pass mfaBackupCodes at all, so the key had to be added: the fix is per-site, not one shared edit.

GET /api/anchors/tx/{txHash} declared — KS-723

What to know.GET /api/anchors/tx/:txHash has been routed since services/anchoring/src/index.ts:1089 and answers { success: true, data: <one anchor> }, but it was not declared in the published OpenAPI document.
An undeclared operation is invisible offline: the contract suites have nothing to generate against it, and a change to its response shape breaks no test.
It is now registered on the shared registry, so npm run generate-openapi emits it. This is narrowing, not closing — one of ~157 routed-but-undocumented operations.
The sibling Stuart named on the same ticket, GET /api/anchors/{id} (index.ts:827), is deliberately left out: declaring it here would make the gateway answer 405 to two live POST routes sharing the prefix.
KS-723 stays open for it.

/revoke is one atomic transition — KS-1278

What to know.POST /api/documents/:id/revoke decided “already revoked” on a READ and then ran an unconditional UPDATE, so two overlapping revokes could both pass the read: 200 + 200, two updates, two action_provenance rows, two anchor calls.
The decision now lives IN the UPDATE — updateDocument’s new ifStatusIsNot option adds AND (… IS NULL OR status IS DISTINCT FROM …) to the WHERE, and a guarded write that changed no row returns null.
The loser answers the same 400 and returns before recordOnBehalfOf, so it records nothing and anchors nothing. Refs KS-1278; the row-lock alternative is not taken.

Round 2 (this commit). The guarded UPDATE still keyed on external_id, but both reads fall back to the primary key for a UUID-shaped id (documentRepo.ts:244-251) and the API hands clients that key as documentUuid (:172).
So a sequential, non-race revoke of a live, owned, unrevoked document addressed by its documentUuid matched 0 rows and answered 400 “already revoked” (gate65 N-1393-1).
The guarded write now keys on the row the read resolved (:616), mirroring the read’s own precedence.
Only the one opted-in caller changes key; the other 12 production updateDocument( callers take a byte-identical statement.

An unreadable connector allow-list fails closed — KS-1256

What to know. The connector allow-list is read from platform settings to decide whether a caller may create a restricted document type.
When the read failed, the parse left connectorConfig = {}, and an empty config reads as no restriction — so every restricted connector became unrestricted exactly when the platform could not see its own restrictions.
It now answers 503 and refuses the create. Two unreadable states, two different mechanisms. A THROWN read (the client’s MaxRetriesPerRequestError, or JSON.parse on malformed stored bytes) is closed in the catch.
But when Redis is UNAVAILABLE, getNotificationSettings resolves null without throwing out of an empty in-memory store — a catch can never see that, so the availability test must run BEFORE the read. Redis up and the key absent is still “no restriction”, by Kam’s ruling b: a fresh install has no key, and failing closed there would refuse every connector create on a new deployment.
A malformed container still answers 403, unchanged. The cost is accepted, not hidden (Kam, card …-1006 = a): while K’s Redis is down, Platform-S cannot create documents through a connector. Watch out: fallbackMode is set by retryStrategy at services/redis.ts:78 with no NODE_ENV guard, so production reached the old fail-open state on a runtime blip despite refusing to START without Redis.
One cell pins that under NODE_ENV=production.

The tenant-isolation job keeps stderr out of its JSON artefact — KS-1436

What to know. jobs/06-tenant-isolation.sh:71 used > "$OUT" 2>&1, so the runner's console.error landed inside 06-tenant-isolation.json.
It is a routine path, not an error path: runner.ts:150-151 deliberately falls back verifier@ → holder@, and the first failed login prints to stderr.
The artefact was then unparseable, the job printed tested=? cross-tenant_findings=? and still exited 0.
Now 2> "$OUT.stderr" — kept, not discarded.
Why the order matters. Before KS 1136 an unreadable artefact read as a CLEAN scan, so a real cross-tenant leak on a fallback run was invisible.
After KS 1136 it reads as unreadable, which reds every routine fallback run with a false cause.
This fix therefore merges FIRST and is that pull request's merge condition.
KS 1382 is a different line in the same file (:31).
Gotcha when you run this locally. No stack is needed — node, curl and tsx are all stubs on a private PATH.
But run bash -n before trusting any red: a syntax error reds every cell with no message.
And check the exec bit: git apply drops it while the index still reads 100755, so the job will not run until chmod 755.

An unparseable security artefact is reported, not read as clean — KS-1136

What to know. 09-aggregate-report.sh read each job's artefact with jq -r … 2>/dev/null (08 with a bare jq), so an artefact that EXISTS but does not PARSE — a job that aborted mid-write — gave zero findings and rc 0, which is indistinguishable from a clean scan.
KS 878 had guarded 04's artefact only.
Each present, unparseable artefact across the seven other jobs (01 02 03 05 06 07 08) now emits its own HIGH finding.
The insertion sits before ALL_FINDINGS reads $TMP, so the findings land in the aggregate rather than after it.
Pure insertion, numstat 14 0; no threshold, baseline, exit-code path or report writer is touched.
Gotcha when you run this locally. Two of them. (1) git apply drops the executable bit. 09-aggregate-report.sh is 100755; after applying the patch git ls-files -s still reads 100755 while the file on disk is -rw-r--r-- and [ -x ] is FALSE.
Re-assert it with chmod 755 on that one file — authorised by the index mode, and prove it changed no byte (cmp rc 0).
Never checkout-index -a. (2) jq -e . takes its exit status from the output VALUE, not only from parseability, so a valid JSON null or false also reads as unreadable.
That is the chosen behaviour, named rather than discovered later.

The format gate’s “in this push” label is literal — KS-998

What to know. check-package-format.sh asked whether a red file was in the current push with grep -qx "$full". -x anchors the match but still treats the path as a regex.
So a red src/a.b.ts that was NOT in the push matched a src/aXb.ts that was, and the gate printed the red as “(in this push)” — the bill landed on the wrong author.
A genuine src/[id].ts IN the push matched nothing and was labelled inherited.
The predicate is now grep -qxF. Only the label changes — the pass/fail verdict, the exit code and the failure count are all unchanged, and three of the eight cells are CONTROLs that assert so.
Gotcha when you run this locally. The new suite is 100644, not 100755, because the payload carries no new file mode line — and that is fine here.
run-shell-suites.sh globs *.test.sh and invokes bash <file>, so it needs no executable bit; package_format_gate.test.sh beside it is 100644 as well. Do not chmod it to match the other 15 suites in that directory — that would be a mode change nothing asks for.
Expect the shell-suite denominator to read 71 at this head, not 70: any older count quoted in a CI triage line does not describe it.

A child run’s verdict never comes from its text — KS-1313

What to know. childSuiteCounts used to regex the child vitest’s human output for Tests N failed | M passed (T). The pattern requires the word “passed”, so fail-only, skip-only and todo-only runs matched nothing and it threw.
And a child killed by its 180 s spawnSync timeout prints no summary, so the read depended on whatever else was in its output — that is the KS 1326 half.
It now passes --reporter=json --outputFile.json=<tmp> and reads numPassedTests / numFailedTests.
endedNormally refuses a spawn error, a signal, or any exit status other than 0 or 1 before the report is read, so a killed child cannot read as a pass even beside a passing report.
Gotcha when you run this locally. The R6 fixtures say “captured from real vitest 4.1.9” and the runner you will resolve is 5.0.3. Check, do not assume: capture a report with the same flags and confirm the fields are still there.
They are — all five the fixtures use are present and numeric in 5.0.3 — but read the version out of node_modules/vitest/package.json, not out of the manifest: ^5.0.3 is a range, not a resolution.
Type-check with tsconfig.node.json. tsconfig.json does not include this file, so a green from it says nothing about this change — confirm with tsc --listFilesOnly if you doubt it.

The transfer reject schema declares signature, so a wrong-typed one is refused — KS-1435

What to know. rejectTransferSchema in services/transfer/src/index.ts never declared signature, while the published TransferRejectRequest declares it a string.
Zod strips keys a schema does not name, so a wrong-typed signature was discarded rather than refused: the schema then validated and the reject ran with 200.
The caller gets a success for a request the contract calls invalid, and the rejection is recorded without the signature it believed it sent.
The fix is one line, signature: z.string().optional(). The narrowing is the type, never the presence — optional() is what keeps every existing caller that sends no signature green.
Pure declaration; no handler branch, no status-code path and no spec file is touched.
Gotcha when you run this locally. Three of them. (1) --reporter=basic no longer exists in vitest 4. It fails as Failed to load custom Reporter from basic with rc 1 and zero tests run — which reads exactly like a red suite if you only look at the exit code. Use the default reporter.
(2) Re-applying the test section SUCCEEDS. git apply --check -v reports Hunk #1 succeeded at 938 (offset -39 lines), because the hunk's only context is two closing-brace lines that recur all over a test file. Do not use "applied twice refuses" as an idempotence guard on this payload; assert the joined-payload sha and the write-tree result instead.
(3) The runner version is in node_modules, not the manifest. It resolves to vitest 4.1.11 where package.json says ^4.1.9.

Transfer-custody: newHolderId is declared a uuid in the published contract — KS-591

What to know. TransferCustodyRequestSchema in services/originate/src/originate.openapi.ts declared newHolderId: z.string().optional() — a plain string for what is a user id — so the published spec advertised any string as valid and Schemathesis scored the operation as accepting bad data.
Now z.string().uuid().optional(). This registry schema is the contract (the spec is generated from it), so declaration and document cannot drift.
The narrowing is the format, never the presence: optional() is kept and newHolderEmail is untouched, so the email-resolution path is unaffected.
KS-591 is a REGISTER (734 failures across 64 operations). This is one operation's one field, so it is a narrowing and the ticket does not move to Done.
Gotcha when you run this locally. Three of them. (1) This service is jest, not vitest. A vitest invocation against services/originate reports 96 failed (96) with Tests: no tests — 0 executed. That is a load failure, and it looks exactly like a catastrophically red suite if you read only the exit code.
(2) Run check:openapi BEFORE regenerating. With the product change in and the YAML untouched it exits 1 with CHECK FAIL: generated YAML differs from on-disk version. That is the only cheap way to prove the later rc 0 is a comparison that ran rather than a gate that cannot fail.
(3) generate-openapi rebuilds packages/shared first (it is npm run build --workspace=packages/shared && tsx scripts/generate-openapi.ts), so a stale dist silently changes what the spec is generated from.

What to know. Job 04 records a scan as failed when trivy reports neither Results nor ArtifactName — KS-1274

What to know. A trivy that exits 0 and prints a bare {} used to be recorded as a clean image. KS 1136 covered a non-zero exit and unparseable output; it did not cover a parseable empty object. The guard at Blockchain/Testing/jobs/04-container-trivy.sh:115-120 now records scan-failed when stdout carries neither Results nor ArtifactName.
Both keys must be absent. Requiring Results alone — which is what the ticket suggests — fails a genuinely clean image that reports only ArtifactName. The suite pins this with a control cell that stays green in the patched and reverted states alike.
The three suites' clean stub moved from {} to {"Results":[]} in the same commit, because under the new guard {} is the failure case. fail.txt accepts bare and nores alongside empty and partial.
Gotcha when you run this locally. (1) docker and trivy are never run. All three suites put stubs on a private PATH; the trivy stub reads $root/fail.txt. So a green run here says nothing about the real scanner — a live run is owed and the ticket stays open.
(2) The exec bit. This repo sets core.filemode=false, and git apply does not restore the executable bit on disk. 04-container-trivy.sh is 100755 in the tree but lands non-executable after a patch, so the suites cannot invoke it until you chmod 755 it. Because filemode is off, git diff shows no mode change either way — read the mode off the committed tree with ls-tree, never from git status.

api-gateway 500 bodies no longer carry the thrown error's text — KS-1410

What to know. Ten catch blocks in routes/notifications.ts, routes/batch.ts and routes/audit-export.ts built their 500 body from err.message with no NODE_ENV guard. Each router now has a fail500 helper that logs the thrown text server-side and answers a constant INTERNAL_ERROR body.
Scope: 10 of the ticket's 28 sites. The remaining 18 are still OPEN, including transfer/delegations.ts, so do not read this as the ticket being finished.
Offline evidence only: base 21 failed | 6 passed (27) → 27 passed, service suite 845 passed. A live run is owed and the ticket does not move to Done.
Gotcha. The six cells that pass at the base too are the 200-path controls. If a future change makes all 27 pass at the base, the suites have stopped discriminating — check the red arm before trusting the green one.
The helper is per-router by design, matching originate's existing shape rather than introducing a shared one; a shared helper is a separate change and is not in this PR.

smoke-test.sh counters no longer abort the script under errexit — KS-1139

What to know. pass(), fail() and warn() counted with ((PASS++)). A post-increment from 0 returns status 1, and under set -euo pipefail bash >= 4.1 applies errexit to (( )) — so the script died at its FIRST check on Linux runners and the Ubuntu demo VM. Now X=$((X + 1)).
It passed on macOS because /bin/bash 3.2.57 ignores that status. A green local run was never evidence here — that is the whole trap.
Evidence: base 3 passed, 3 failed -> 6 passed, 0 failed; sibling smoke_test_degraded_warns.test.sh 5 passed, 0 failed. The three cells passing in both arms are the controls.
Gotcha. Unreproducible on this host: no bash >= 4.1 exists here, so the red-before-fix behaviour is read from documented errexit semantics plus a reviewer's rc-1 figure on bash 5.2. A run on bash >= 4.1 is owed and the ticket does not move to Done.
Scope: the three helpers in smoke-test.sh only. The scripts/validate-env.sh sites of the same shape are still OPEN and deliberately not in this change.

The published OpenAPI yaml is generated, so a Spark companion diff is never applied — KS-1449

What to know. Six nft operations declared a requestBody with no required: true, so a spec-driven caller sent no body and called the request valid; the handler's zod safeParse answered 400 and the sweep failed positive_data_acceptance. All six are now marked required in services/nft-certificate/src/nft-certificate.openapi.ts.
The published YAML is GENERATED. Patch the .openapi.ts and run npm run generate-openapi in Blockchain/Dev; never hand-edit docs/openapi/secuura-api.yaml and never git apply a Spark openapi-yaml.companion.diff. Those companions are cut against spec blob 52310cab1 and at a +87-line develop five of twelve apply rc 0 onto the WRONG operation, because their context is just requestBody: / content: / application/json:.
Spec half only: each handler's own 400 on an absent body is READ, not driven — no local stack. The four platform suites are UNMEASURED. The ticket does not move to Done.
Gotcha when you run this locally. (1) Prove the YAML structurally, not by diff size. Parse base and head and compare every paths[p][m].requestBody.required; a line-count check cannot tell a required: true on the right operation from one on its neighbour. Here: exactly 6 flipped, 0 off, 0 operations added or removed, 6 added lines in 40,021.
(2) A hunk offset that is not the file's drift is a mislanding. This spec drifted +1 at line 6910 and +86 at 14465, so a correctly-placed hunk reports an offset of 0, 1 or 87. Across the twelve companions every correct one sits in {1, 87} and every mislander carries one outside it (11, 6, 10, 13, 26). Cheap tripwire, and necessary not sufficient — a wrong anchor exactly 87 lines on would pass it, so keep the structural reader as the gate.

The absent-anchor 60 s boundary is pinned INCLUSIVE at the constant — KS-1171

What to know. Rule (c) of the absent-anchor decision in services/anchoring/src/anchorSubmission.ts:345 reads lastAnsweredElapsedMs >= ABSENT_MIN_LAST_ANSWER_MS, where the constant is 60_000 ms. That comparison is the point at which a second fee-paying Cardano transaction may be minted, and nothing asserted whether the constant itself counts as absent.
This row is a pin on an existing money path, not a change to it: no product line moves, and anchorSubmission.ts is byte-identical before and after. It settles gate finding B1 of #1228 only — the 8k item and the live sweep stay owed, and KS-1171 stays open.
Three cells: the last answer exactly at the constant (with enough answered polls) is absent and a retry is scheduled; one millisecond earlier is not absent; and the two constants read 2 and 60000.
How to red it. A pin has no red at its base, so do not look for one. Change >= to > at anchorSubmission.ts:345 — prove the anchor string is unique first (grep -c reads 1; the bare constant name reads 2 as its control) — re-run, then restore by content and assert the whole-file sha256 against the pre-tamper hash. A real red reports 3 total / 1 failed; a 0 total / 0 failed reading is a load failure and proves nothing.

run-migrations.sh reports skipped migrations as skipped, not applied — KS-808

What to know. The summary of Blockchain/Dev/scripts/run-migrations.sh is now Summary: applied=A failed=F skipped=S. Before, apply_one returned 0 for an already applied skip as well as for a real apply, so applied= counted skips: a re-run over an up-to-date database reported every migration as applied.
Only the counter moved. Exit codes are unchanged (any failed migration still exits 3, KS 1031), and Migration runner finished (applied=…, failed=…) keeps its format with the true apply count. KS-808 stays open: a live run against a real database is owed.

Point RUN_MIGRATIONS_SH at a copy of the pre-change runner to see the old behaviour: 3 passed, 2 failed.

(3) The runner is baked into the migrations image. A local stack only picks this change up after migrations is rebuilt; rsyncing the script alone changes nothing.

stack_guard prints one line per project, even when its containers disagree about the owner — KS-1355

What to know. stack_guard.sh grouped foreign stacks by (project, owner), so a project whose containers carried different owner labels was listed once per owner. It now prints one line per project.
Where some containers have a real owner and others are owner=unknown, the line names the real owner and appends (+N container(s) with owner unknown). That wording is this change's choice, not the ticket's.
Behaviour change: a project with two different real owners now prints ONE line — the first tuple in docker ps order — so the second real owner is no longer shown. NARROWING: spot 1 only; KS-1355 does NOT close.

transfer-custody resolves a holder email inside originate, so S's connector can name a holder by email — KS-1402

What to know. POST /api/documents/:id/transfer-custody with newHolderEmail no longer calls auth's GET /api/users/lookup with the caller's credential. Originate hashes the email (auth's own toLowerCase().trim()) and reads users on the request's db with the tenant bound. Platform S's connector carries no users:read, so it was refused 403 every time; it now resolves.
The guard to watch is the tenant. A connector can now reach resolution, so the bound tenant predicate is what keeps it out of another tenant: a tenant-B-only email is 404, byte-equal to no user at all (cells C2 and C2b). The act's own gate (documents:transfer-custody or an allowed role) is unchanged and runs first.
Answers. Malformed email 400 (same body as before); no user in your tenant 404 RECIPIENT_NOT_FOUND; originate without a lookup key 502 BAD_GATEWAY, labelled. The old 401/403/502 that came from the lookup (KS 739) cannot occur on this route any more.
Gotcha when you run this locally. (1) Originate now needs PII_LOOKUP_HMAC_KEY. Without it every email transfer answers 502, and a unit suite that drives the email path must register a key first: that is the one line ks697 gained.
(2) Run the WHOLE suite (npm test in services/originate): 97 suites / 1096 tests / 0 failed (base 96 / 1093), measured 2026-10-09 on Kamils-Mac-Studio, node v24.7.0. Three per-file runs were green while the whole suite was not: the KS 1061 guard requires every @secuura/shared mock to be () => require('./helpers/sharedModuleMock').makeSharedMock(, matched as text, so a block-bodied factory still fails it.
(3) A tamper that does not compile reds nothing. ts-jest type-checks, so a tamper that leaves a variable unread (TS6133) is a load failure with 0 passed and 0 failed, not a red. Read the executed count before calling a tamper proven.

api-gateway fail500 logs a thrown object's type and field names, so a non-Error throw is no longer [object Object] — KS-1346

What to know. Three api-gateway routers share one fail500() helper (routes/notifications.ts, routes/batch.ts, routes/audit-export.ts). Its log line was String(err), so a thrown non-Error object became "[object Object]" and its content was lost. It now logs the thrown value's type and field names.

transfer process-expired: the 500 body carries a constant and the thrown text goes to the log — KS-1410

What to know. POST /api/delegations/admin/process-expired used to answer 500 with message: error.message. If you were reading the response body to find out what broke, read the service log instead.
Which branch logs. The log call is inside the handler's if (error instanceof Error) branch, so that branch is what logs error.message. A non-Error throw takes next(error) and never reaches the line, so do not read this row as a statement about non-Error throws elsewhere in the service.

ApiKeyCreateRequest declares the rotate flag POST /api/security/keys acts on — KS-1434

What to know. rotate is optional and defaults to false. It does nothing without a connectorId: both are needed before any key is retired.
The keys retired are the other active keys for that connector in the same tenant. The query does not look at who created a key.
With API_KEY_ROTATION_GRACE_SECONDS unset or 0 they are deactivated; with a positive value they are given an expiry that many seconds away if that is earlier than their current one.
The 201 response carries priorKeysRevoked only when both rotate and connectorId were sent. null means the retirement was attempted and failed, and the prior key is still valid.
Gotcha when you run this locally. This change is the published contract only. Nothing in the running service changes, and a gateway that has already read the old spec keeps serving it until it is restarted.
Not measured here: the spec as served by a running gateway, and any effect on a Schemathesis sweep now that the field is visible to it.

Leg 14’s comment filter is pinned by a fixture hook carrying a commented control — KS-1746

What to know. preflight.sh:722 drops comment lines before leg 14 extracts the pre-push hook’s ${NAME:- control reads, so a control named only in a comment is never stripped from the suite run. On the shipped hook the only match is live code at .githooks/pre-push:77, so the filter changes nothing there — which is why the gate’s mutant G2 (delete the filter) survived preflight_deps.test.sh at 68/0.
Test file only: a third hook-stub mode (commented) on the existing preflight_fixture_run fixture, one extra reporting line in the fixture stub suite, and seven cells. preflight.sh is unchanged, sha256 identical before and after.
Two oracles. The announcement (the strip line must read exactly ALLOW_FORCE, asserted whole-line) and the behaviour read from the stripped side (FAKE=1 in the caller’s environment must still reach the suite). The second exists because the announcement is what mutant M3 proved leg 14 can fake.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Drive G2 as a matched pair, not a single arm. Six cells in this suite read above DEV_ROOT ($DEV_ROOT/../../.githooks/pre-push, the four slot-gateway cells, Projects Documents/PORT-REGISTRY.md), so pointing PREFLIGHT_DEPS_TEST_ROOT at a bare copy of Blockchain/Dev reddens them by relocation alone and reads 48/9 — three times G2’s real effect. Lay the copy out as a repo root (.githooks/, systemTest/, Projects Documents/ beside Blockchain/Dev) and run an unmutated control arm beside the mutant.
FAKE must be SET for the behavioural cell to mean anything. env -u on an already-unset variable is indistinguishable from not stripping it, so an unset probe reads <unset> on both arms. One cell pins that vacuity on purpose; do not “simplify” the FAKE=1 out of the arm above it. Also: the commented line in a fixture hook must start with # — a ${NAME:- in a trailing comment is not excluded by the filter.
Not measured here. The nit-3 read shapes (${NAME}, ${NAME:?}, bare $NAME) are still not stripped; filed as KS 1755. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED, and no service unit suite was run because no service source is touched. Nothing on either box.
Noted, not fixed: the pre-existing nocontrol fixture mode does not model an empty derivation — its hook line does not start with #, so NAME is derived and the refusal fires on the missing ALLOW_FORCE instead. Same guard branch, so the cell still measures a real refusal.

generated_guard_restore keeps the suite's exit status when a cleanup is chained in front — KS-1738

What to know. generated_guard_restore() reads the suite’s status as its first statement (local rc=$?). A cleanup chained in FRONT of it runs first and overwrites $?, so a suite that exited 3 was reported as 1. LOW: the failed restore is still loud and still non-zero; only “the suite failed” versus “the restore failed” is lost.
Fix is opt-in: the guard now prefers a status passed as its first argument over $?, so every plain-trap caller (including the two inline copies) is unchanged. The chained call site becomes trap 'rc=$?; stop_stub; generated_guard_restore "$rc"' EXIT. A non-numeric argument is refused by name.
Do NOT put the guard first (generated_guard_restore $?; stop_stub), which is what the ticket literally suggested: on a failed restore the guard exits, so the chained cleanup would never run on exactly the path the guard exists for — it would leak the stub listener.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Drive this in three arms, each a distinct (suite, guard) pair, and fingerprint them. The guard is a sourced file installing an EXIT trap, so a cell cannot test it in-process: build scratch trees of <root>/generated_guard_restore.test.sh + <root>/support/generated_guard.sh, because the suite derives the guard path from its own location and has no override knob. The RED arm is the NEW suite against the ORIGINAL guard.
B stays at 1 after the fix, and that is correct. Shape 1 is opt-in, so a chained call site passing no status still flattens it. If you expect B to flip you will read a correct run as a failure. Each arm points $FIXTURES at its own throwaway directory; no cell touches the repo’s real systemTest/fixtures/generated.
Not measured here. The two INLINE copies (pre_suite.test.sh, quarantine_call_sites.test.sh) are untouched — both use a plain trap, so neither is affected. A grep for generated_guard_restore at this tip finds only the three files this change touches, and nothing wider is claimed.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED; no service unit suite was run, because no service source is touched. Nothing on either box.

The M365 connection status enum is pinned in all three declarations — KS-1754

The four values are declared three times: the stored TS union at services/m365-integration/src/index.ts:94 and two z.enum copies in m365-integration.openapi.ts — :89 in M365InitConnectionResponse and :114 in M365ConnectionSchema. Nothing asserted they agree; KS-1700 fixed the values and this pins them.
The pin asserts the COUNT as well as the agreement, because a pin against M365ConnectionSchema alone would leave the :89 copy free. A fourth declaration fails the count cell on purpose, so it has to be added to the pin deliberately.
A test only — no source, spec or yaml change. It reads the two files as TEXT (no export needed) and strips comments string-aware, so a commented-out enum cannot satisfy it (the KS-1319 class); KS1754_INDEX_TS / KS1754_OPENAPI_TS point it at copies.
Not measured here. Anything at runtime: no handler is exercised, and what the service stores or returns is untouched by this change. The 'active' DDL default outside the enum is KS-1752 item 2, a product call, not this ticket.
