The “run compared as one value” guard and the REAL cell’s summary — KS-1529

What to know. The ks1000 file’s guard against comparing a checker run as ONE value (status and output inside one array or object) used to be a per-line regex on the array form. It missed an object, a reversed array and an array split over several lines; it is now runsComparedAsOneValue, a scan of the joined text bounded by ] or }. A bound of ) would miss a nested call ahead of the first field, so a cell plants that shape.
The REAL cell’s expected summary is a named constant, AUTH_GREEN_SUMMARY. A cell proves it can fail (a red run, a silent run with the right status, a wrong-status run), and a second cell reads the REAL cell off the syntax tree to prove it still passes the constant. Both are needed: with the second cell excluded, the gate’s M3 (the REAL cell cut to a bare status check) leaves the whole file green, 83 passed and 0 red.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Plant a shape by assembly, never literally. The guard scans this file’s whole text, strings and comments included, so a literal expect( followed by [ or { and both fields flags the file itself. The cells build the plants from 'expect' + '('. A permitted shape is the file’s own idiom (expect(r.status, r.out) appears in it already), so those cells count occurrences added, not occurrences present.
A mutant copy must sit beside the test, and under src/__tests__ it is type-checked by the real ratchet. The cells read __filename, so a copy reads itself; but the REAL cell runs the checker over services/auth, copy included, so a mutant that leaves an unused name reds the REAL cell with TS6133 for that reason and not because of the cell’s logic. Delete every copy and check git status.
The -t KS-1529 filter runs only the new describe. The rewritten guard cell is in the KS 1519 describe and the REAL cell in the KS 1000 one, so run the whole file to see them. Under load the full suite also times out ks949 (5 s test timeout) and auth.integration (10 s HOOK timeout): raise --testTimeout AND --hookTimeout, they are different knobs.
Not measured here. A bracket nested ahead of the second field (parts[0], a nested object literal) is NOT caught — measured, 0 hits — and only the REAL cell is protected of the 35 expectRun call sites at base (39 after). The predicate is a substring: 10 new; 0 stale baseline entries contains it, though no run with new errors exits 0.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED; nothing on either box.

The two arms of leg 14’s hook-control refusal are told apart by their Derived line — KS-1756

What to know. preflight.sh:731 refuses to run the shell suites when the derived set of the pre-push hook’s controls is empty or is non-empty but lacks ALLOW_FORCE. Both classify as HOOKCTL-REFUSED/1, so only the refusal’s own Derived: [...] line tells them apart. preflight_deps.test.sh now drives each arm with its own fixture mode — nocontrol (hook line starts with #, set is empty) and trailingcomment (the old line, NAME derived) — and asserts the line as a whole (grep -cxF).
Until this change nocontrol claimed the empty set and modelled the other arm, so a condition that let an empty derivation through passed the suite at 75/0. No product file changes.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Do not try to make a cell go red when the -z disjunct is deleted. It is redundant: for an empty set the second half of the condition is also true, so the deletion refuses on exactly the same inputs (a five-row truth table under bash 3.2 with pipefail; the matched-pair run reads 82/0 with zero cells moved). The cells pin the two mutants that are killable: M_accept (an empty set is accepted) by the empty arm, M_2nd (only -z remains) by the trailing-comment arm.
Drive mutants as a matched pair on a repo-root layout. Six cells in this suite read above DEV_ROOT, so a bare copy of Blockchain/Dev reddens them by relocation alone. git archive <base> | tar -x -C <dir> gives a complete layout; run an unmutated control beside each mutant and apply every mutation as an exact-string replace that aborts unless its anchor occurs exactly once.
The empty-arm hook line carries a ${NAME:- inside a # comment on purpose. Deleting the comment filter (G2) then derives NAME and turns the arm’s Derived line from [ ] to [NAME ], so G2 reddens two of these cells as well as KS-1746’s three. A hook line that is merely empty would not do that.
Not measured here. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED, and no service unit suite was run because no service source is touched. Nothing on either box. The redundant -z disjunct is left in preflight.sh.

The ks1186 test runs every control against every site, and listUsers classifies a DB fault — KS-1484

What to know. The ks1186 test now drives all fifteen cells from one table of the five sites (getUserByIdWithPasswordHash, getUserByIdPreAuth, getUserByEmail hash arm, getUserByEmail legacy arm, getUserBySocialId): an infrastructure fault becomes 503 and is logged; a non-infrastructure fault is rethrown as the very same error (toBe, not a message match) and logged; with no fault the row resolves and nothing is logged as an error.
Blockchain/Dev/services/auth/src/repositories/userRepo.ts:372-373 no longer says the five reads are unawaited. That edit is comment-only, proved by a comment-stripped AST hash equal on both sides, with three controls that fire.
Item 3 is a BEHAVIOUR CHANGE. listUsers now has the lookups’ contract: one try over the SELECT and the per-row fromRow reads; it logs DB listUsers failed; an infrastructure fault is a ServiceUnavailableError (503), anything else is rethrown as the very same error. On GET /api/users and GET /api/users/admin/list that is a 503 where it was a 500 (read, not run). The await on Promise.all is load-bearing: without it the rejection escapes the try, which is the KS 1186 bug class.
Gotchas when you run this locally. getUserByEmail has two arms and the default mock reaches only one. The default query mock answers the hash query with no rows, so a bare call goes to the legacy arm. The hash arm needs the first query answer primed (mockResolvedValueOnce with the row); the table’s prime hook does it. A cell that forgets this tests the legacy arm twice and says nothing about the hash arm.
A mutant of a production file needs both the repo and the test copied. The test imports ../repositories/userRepo by a dynamic import(); a mutant copy sits in the same directory (same relative imports) and each test copy has that one import repointed. Run the OLD test (the base blob) beside the NEW one: a mutant that survives the old and is killed by the new is the proof a control was widened, not merely renamed.
listUsers needs no extra mock. runWithPlatformScope comes from @secuura/shared, which this test mocks with ...actual, so the real one runs. The first query answer is the SELECT; prime it with mockResolvedValueOnce({ rows, rowCount }). A resolved subject DEK must be a Buffer (Buffer.from('test-dek')): getDek returns one, and a string is a TS2345 the type-check ratchet reports as a NEW error.
A mutant that does not parse reds every cell and looks like a spectacular kill. The item-3 driver parses each mutant with the TypeScript compiler API and refuses one that does not. One of mine had a stray closing brace and reded all 23 cells when the code measured directly gave 4.
Not measured here. countUsersInner has the same gap and is not touched (userRepo.ts:1092, one query, no try/catch; both list routes call it right after listUsers), so a fault on the COUNT still answers a raw 500. The route path (catch (error) { next(error); } then the error handler) was read, not run, and every database call in these cells is a mock.
A runtime change that is NOT deployed: under the Done rule the ticket cannot move to Done until auth is. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED; nothing on either box.

What to know. In ks1634-v2-chain-only-match-carries-simulated.test.ts the two N3 cells send simulated as the string "true" and as the number 1, each with a real tx hash on purpose: a mock_tx_ hash makes the presenter refuse the reply by itself, which would hide the === true mutant. The KS-1347 proxy probe inherits server.ts’s imports by statement, so the respellings it reads no longer red it — KS-1560

What to know. ks1347_proxy_out_dir_spaced_checkout.test.sh copies server.ts’s node: imports into a probe so that deleting one reds the suite. They were read with a one-line grep that matched only import X from 'node:[a-z]+'; at the end of a line. Double quotes, a missing semicolon, extra spaces, a wrapped list, a trailing comment, CRLF or node:path/posix each made the set incomplete and reddened four cells while server.ts worked.
The grep is now extract_node_imports, written to the fixture directory and run on the real subject and on eight synthetic spellings. 20/0 from 8/0. A real deletion of an import still reds, and the control that the set is non-empty and names node:path and node:url still fires on an emptied list. server.ts is not edited.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Respell a COPY, in a mini-tree. The suite reads its subject only as $HERE/../openapi-examples/proxy/server.ts, so scripts/__tests__/<suite> beside scripts/openapi-examples/proxy/server.ts in a temp directory is a complete harness. Run the old and the new suite on the same copy; the old suite reads 4/4 on every respelling of an import the expression needs.
A respelling only reds the old suite when it lands on an import the expression needs. A trailing comment on fs left it green at 8/0 because the expression does not use fs. Put the respelling on path or url. And node --check cannot parse TypeScript here (const x: string fails), so validate a respelling on the import block alone as a .mjs file; the suite does this and has a control that a broken import is rejected.
Count rows against arms. A matrix driver that reads its arm list with while read silently drops the last arm if the file has no trailing newline. The first run printed 10 rows for 11 arms and was caught only by that comparison.
Not measured here. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED; no service unit suite was run; nothing on either box. Two KS-1355 guard survivors closed: the launcher’s .env-then-stack_env.sh order, and the compose checker’s website origin on every list — KS-1558 m1, m2

What to know. m2: start-local.sh sources .env then stack_env.sh so the slot beats a stale port literal. The old fixture .env set no port, so swapping the lines was invisible (23/0 on the tampered launcher). The fixture .env now carries GATEWAY_PORT=9999 and WEBSITE_PORT=9998; the tampered launcher reads 26/4. m1: check-compose-stack-safety.mjs now requires slot 2’s website port in every comma-separated origin list in MUST_MOVE, not api-gateway’s alone.
Suite 23/0 → 36/0 (seven m2 cells, six m1 cells). The checker has no suite of its own, so its cells live in ks1355_slot_blind_launchers.test.sh.
Gotchas when you run this locally. The m1 cells run the real checker with no Docker. It needs the docker compose CLI, not a daemon, and resolves docker from PATH, so a stub docker that prints a canned config --format json drives it. The canned render is a model of what the checker reads, not the real compose file; it proves the logic only.
To check the real render without a daemon, run the checker from the worktree with DOCKER_HOST=unix:///nonexistent.sock: if config reached for the daemon it would fail loudly. Each run makes three config calls (bare, slot 1, slot 2). Do the tamper run on a git archive COPY, and confirm the copy’s compose files are byte-identical to the worktree’s before the tamper.
Tamper the launcher in place, then prove the revert. Move source .env to after the stack_env.sh block, run the base and the new suite against it, then git checkout the file and compare its blob with HEAD’s. Run the base suite from an untracked copy placed beside the new one and delete it after.
Not measured here. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED; no service unit suite was run. m3 and m5 are not in this change. Nothing executes the checker today: only two retired GitHub Actions workflows call it, and check-stack-safety.sh:524 tests existence only.

The document-lookup catch can no longer be taken down by its own log line — KS-1524

What to know. Two things changed on GET /api/platform/document-lookup. The old file passed 9 of 9 with that guard removed; the new file fails L6 alone, 3 of 3 runs.

A thrown value that cannot be described (a throwing message getter, a Proxy whose getPrototypeOf or ownKeys trap throws, a revoked Proxy) or a logger that throws used to reject the handler from its own catch and leave the request with no response. The route now sends the constant 500 in all five cases and logs thrown value that could not be described for the first four.

Akto reports carry no credentials; Akto 2.41.6 — KS-1608

What to know. Every file and page the Akto harness writes is masked through one module, systemTest/akto/src/core/redact.ts: report.json, the HTML report, curl reproducers, service logs, threads.txt and exception messages. Credential headers, JWTs, Bearer tokens, sk_ keys, credential JSON fields and ?token=-style parameters become ***. Ids, hashes, wallet addresses and non-credential headers stay, so a finding is still reproducible. To reproduce with a credential, mint a fresh token on the slot; the report will never hold one.

countUsers classifies a database fault the way listUsers does — KS-1769

What to know. countUsersInner in Blockchain/Dev/services/auth/src/repositories/userRepo.ts now has the catch listUsersInner got in KS 1484. A database fault at the COUNT query is a 503 (SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE) when isInfrastructureDbError says it is an availability fault, anything else is rethrown as the very same error (toBe, not a message match), and every failure is logged once as DB countUsers failed with the error and its code. Before this, GET /api/users and GET /api/users/admin/list answered a raw 500 whenever the list answered and the count faulted, because both routes call the list and then the count.
The twelve new cells sit at the end of services/auth/src/__tests__/ks1186-userrepo-fromrow-sites-are-awaited.test.ts and use its helpers (loadRepo, rejection, INFRA). Four are red against the code before the catch; three pin the result path; five pin that the tenant, search and role filters reach the query as the same SQL and the same bound values, so the catch cannot have moved the WHERE builder.
This is a behaviour change on the user-listing path and it is not deployed: under the Done rule the ticket cannot move to Done until auth is.
Gotchas when you run this locally. The ks949 tree-walking cells time out at the default 5 s in a full run under load. ks949-platform-admin-seed-identity.test.ts read 2 failures on the cold first run in a fresh worktree and 1 on a later run (Test timed out in 5000ms); alone it passes in well under a second. For a load-proof full run pass --testTimeout=120000 --hookTimeout=120000. It is the flake KS 1053 tracks, not a defect of the count.
A route-level driver on tsx cannot assign onto a module. tsx compiles through esbuild, whose CommonJS exports are getter-only, so db.query = fake is silently ignored (the first run of the driver answered 401 on both routes). Replace the cached module’s exports instead (require.cache[path].exports = Object.assign({}, real, { query: fake })), and install the database seam before anything loads that could capture the real one.
A mutant’s test copy must still end in .test.ts. A copy named …test.M1.ts is outside vitest’s include glob, so every mutant would read “no tests ran”. Parse each mutant before running it: a syntax-broken mutant reds every cell and reads as a kill.
Run tsx with a short TMPDIR. It opens a unix socket under it and macOS caps that path near 104 bytes; a TMPDIR under a long scratch path dies with listen EINVAL, which reads like a repo defect. This round used /tmp/xk1769.
Not measured here. Every database call in the cells and in the route run is a mock; a real pg fault was not produced. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) were not run, nothing was run on Node 24, and the Node v26.8.1 used here was not checked against the vendor’s LTS schedule.
KS 1168 (the encrypted-column search) will rewrite the two search clauses the filter controls pin; it has to update those cells in the same change.

The ks1000 test no longer lets a worse summary pass, sees nested brackets, and pins the expectRun call sites — KS-1765

What to know. Three residuals KS-1529 left in services/auth’s ks1000-auth-test-files-are-type-checked-against-a-shrinking-baseline.test.ts are closed, all in that one test file. expectRun’s string predicate is a substring match, so the old green summary 0 new; 0 stale baseline entries was contained in 10 new; 0 stale baseline entries: AUTH_GREEN_SUMMARY is now a RegExp with a lookbehind that refuses a digit directly before 0 new.
The guard that keeps a cell from comparing a checker run as one value now counts bracket depth, so [parts[0], r.status, r.out] is caught, and steps over quoted strings and template literals so a '[' inside the value cannot make it read on into the next statement. Two new cells pin every ordinary expectRun call (a numeric status and a predicate that can fail: not '', not /./) and a floor of 30 ordinary calls.
Adding an expectRun call is fine: the floor is a floor. Deleting one, or replacing it with a bare status check, goes red; lower ORDINARY_CALLS_FLOOR in the same commit only if the deletion is intended. Calls inside expect(() => ...) or thrownBy(() => ...) are the subject of a cell that expects them to throw, and are not counted.
Gotchas when you run this locally. Run the suite with the service directory as the working directory. ks1009 finds the Dev root from process.cwd(); run from the repo root (for example vitest --root from a shell sitting in 2_Project_Files) it fails with could not locate the Dev root, which reads like a regression. npm --prefix Blockchain/Dev run test --workspace=services/auth sets the directory for you.
Under load the default timeouts go red on cells that are not yours. Three ks949 cells (they end at exactly the 5 s default and pass alone, 31 / 31) and the auth.integration hook (10 s, 56 cells skipped) failed at the defaults before and after this change. Pass --testTimeout=120000 --hookTimeout=120000 and the suite is 1188 of 1188. This is the class KS-1155 tracks.
A mutant copy of the ks1000 file under src/__tests__ is type-checked by the ratchet and scanned by the REAL cell. Keep it only while no ratchet check runs and delete it after the run. A copy that reads its own text (SELF) sees the mutant, which is what the guard cells want.
A mutant must parse, and a row must be able to fail. The first form of the escaped-quote row passed with escape handling removed, because the scan still reached the same fields; it only discriminates when a quoted ] follows the escaped quote, so a mis-read string closes the value early.
Not measured here. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance) are UNMEASURED here. Nothing ran on either box. 14 other call sites use a string predicate that starts with a digit and share the substring property; they are named, not changed.
Not proved by the pin: that any predicate is the right one, a predicate swapped for another that can fail, or a removal paired with an addition elsewhere (measured: the cells stay green). Not run on an LTS Node: the host carries only v26.8.1.

api-client.ts declares the error body the gateway sends — KS-1767

What to know. The MCP server’s api-client.ts typed a response’s error as a string at three sites (registerDocument, getWorkflowInstance, getConnectorInfo), while the gateway’s published error body carries an object { code, message }. Nothing checks the declared type at run time, so the type and the wire had drifted apart unseen. The three sites now share one alias, string | { code?: string; message?: string }, and the as unknown as cast in tools/info.ts that worked round the old type is gone.
Read from source: the routes’ own refusals are objects. A flat string exists only in the brute-force limiter’s 429 branch (429 { error: 'Too many failed attempts', ... }), which KS-1372 records as unreachable today because nothing calls its recordFailure. The string arm is kept for the old declaration, for tools/info.ts’s own string test, and for that branch if it is ever wired. The info.ts edit changes no behaviour: its emitted JavaScript is identical line for line.
The new test reads the types, because vitest does not type-check: it compiles a probe against the real api-client.ts through the TypeScript compiler API. 10 cells: per site T1 (the object is assignable), T2 (a string is assignable), C1 (a number is refused), plus C2 (the probe resolved the real file). Red-first: 3 failed / 7 passed on the unfixed file, 10 of 10 fixed. Eight one-line mutants each fail exactly their own cells.
Not measured here. A run on an LTS Node: the host carries only v26.8.1 and nothing was repeated on Node 24. Nothing calls a gateway; the wire reading is from source files, and the reverse proxy in front of the gateway was not read.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance). Three other places that read data.error and relay it (tools/info.ts once, tools/register.ts twice) are not changed.

GET /api/platform/document-lookup is in the published contract, as a public route — KS-1773

What to know. The route was live and missing from the published spec. It is now published as a deliberate public endpoint (security: []; Kam, 2026-10-28: “Keep it public and publish it”) with query parameters hash and documentId and the answers 200, 400, 429, 500 and 503. There is no 404: a lookup that matches nothing is a 200 with an empty results list.
Publishing arms the gateway’s method gate: after a deploy and a gateway restart, every non-GET method on this path, HEAD included, is a 405 with Allow: GET; POST and DELETE were the fall-through 404 and HEAD was answered by the GET handler. Authentication does not move: an anonymous GET still gets the handler’s own 400.
No handler change; the cells compare the handler’s answers with what the yaml declares.
Not measured here. A live platform database (the 200 and 500 bodies come from a stubbed pool; column types are from the init DDL), a running stack, and the gateway’s 429.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing. The change is not deployed.

AppError subclasses in packages/shared keep their own prototype — KS-1467

What to know. Every subclass of AppError in packages/shared (nine, plus DocumentNotFoundError under NotFoundError) used to be reset to the base prototype by the constructor, so err instanceof NotFoundError was always false. The constructor now uses new.target.prototype (one line). Status, code, details and the handler’s answers do not change.
This is the shared half of KS-1467. The services/auth half (its own error classes) is a separate change. A local model wrote the patch; it is applied unchanged.

isPlatformRole and requireKeyAdmin: exact ASCII fold, non-string role refused — KS-1591

What to know. The platform-administrator predicate (isPlatformRole, packages/shared) and the key-admin gate in front of the key routes (requireKeyAdmin, services/security) upper-cased the role with the locale-aware toUpperCase() after stringifying it. A role with U+0131 or U+017F in it, or an array holding an admin role, therefore read as an admin role. Both now apply an ASCII-only fold and refuse a role that is not a string.
Every spelling admitted before (SYSTEM_ADMIN, SUPER_ADMIN, ISSUER_ADMIN and their lower and mixed case forms) is admitted now; the change can only remove access. Two local-model passes, applied unchanged.
Not measured here. Whether such a token can reach either predicate in production, the other importers of isPlatformRole (their suites were not run), and a Node 24 LTS run (the host is Node 26.8.1).
A running stack and the four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing. The change is not deployed.

The IPFS service bounds all eight outbound fetches, and a stalled gateway body answers 503 — KS-1240 part 3

What to know. Eight outbound fetch calls in services/nft-certificate/src/services/ipfs.service.ts had no timeout. Each now passes AbortSignal.timeout: 60 s for the three uploads, 15 s for the four pin and unpin calls, 30 s for the gateway read. On expiry an upload answers { success: false }, pin and unpin answer false, and the gateway read answers 503.
A behaviour change on a read path. The gateway body read now sits inside the try that maps a failure to 503, so a gateway that sends its headers and then stalls (or drops the connection mid-body) answers 503 instead of the raw error. An object the gateway needs more than 30 s to deliver now answers 503 where it used to wait.
Scope. The ticket text names only the api-gateway key-validate fetch; this part was added by the coordinator’s brief, not by the ticket.
Not measured here. A real provider endpoint, a real network connect timeout, the routes that call the service, and a running stack. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are UNMEASURED and not claimed as a pass. The change is not deployed.
Node v26.8.1, not the repository’s stated LTS line of Node 24 (none is installed on this host). The figures are declared as measured on v26.8.1, not claimed as LTS.

The published OAuth authorize contract names the OAuth token and the role-or-scope door — KS-1472

What to know. The sentence in both /api/oauth/authorize descriptions now says “An OAuth token with no scopes” (a plain login JWT passes requireScope on RBAC, so only an OAuth token is refused), adds “unless the route also admits the caller by role” (requireOrgProvisioner admits SUPER_ROLES with no scope), and says “requires no scope” where it said “requires none”. The wording lives in auth.openapi.ts; the yaml is regenerated, never hand-edited.
The route that requires no scope “does not refuse it for want of one, though a role check on that route can still refuse it”. The first wording said such a route “accepts any valid token”, which the QA gate flagged as false for a role-gated route: requireSuperAdmin answers 403 outside SUPER_ROLES, and GET /api/platform/tenants has no requireScope.
The pin is STATES_THE_TOKEN in ks839-contract-states-the-wildcard-allow-list-rule.test.ts; the false-claim guard beside it is unchanged and still red when the claim “the gateway answers 403 on it” is added. The ks1210 header now names redirectUris only, which is all its control reads.
Not measured here. A request through a running gateway: the cells read the yaml text and send nothing. What the sentence says about the gateway was read from scopes.ts and platform.ts, not run.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing, and Node 24 was not run. The new text is served only after a deploy and a gateway restart.

The published document operations state the reserved metadata keys, and the real-Postgres file pins the strip before the hash — KS-1537

What to know. Four operations now carry one sentence on the keys a caller cannot choose. POST /api/documents silently STRIPS data.versionedById before hashing. POST /:id/version, /:id/sign-cert and /:id/sign-wallet reserve versionAction, versionedAt, versionedById and parentDocumentId: the server sets them after metadata, so a value you supply is overwritten. The pin is ks1537-the-contract-states-the-reserved-metadata-keys.test.ts; the yaml is regenerated, never hand-edited.
ks1508-a-planted-versionedbyid-is-not-stored.integration.test.ts goes from 6 to 12 cells on a real Postgres, in both tenancy modes: hash and hash2 pin the strip to come BEFORE the content hash and the thread-token hash; the certify cells plant the key in POST /api/certifications/issue as JSON, form-encoded and as a __proto__ payload. The route needs no stubbed collaborators; the old header said it did.
The integration cells need a migrated database and three URLs (TEST_DATABASE_URL, TEST_APP_DATABASE_URL as secuura_app, and TEST_PLATFORM_DATABASE_URL for pool mode). Run the migration runner with the main URL only, so the platform-routed file 003 lands in the one database. N-4 (should the certify response, record and hash input also drop the value) is a decision and is not built.
The erase-connector description no longer says a writer can still hide a derivative “until the server sets versionedById”: it says the server sets it itself, a value supplied on create or on a certify is dropped, and a row stored before that change keeps whatever it was written with. One guard cell in the pin keeps the stale phrases out and the corrected sentence in. The same stale claim is still in a code comment in connectorOrganisationErasure.ts (line 209), reported and not edited here.
Not measured here. A running gateway or stack, anchoring on a chain, the gateway’s 429, and Node 24. The mint is recorded, not performed. The new yaml text is served only after a deploy and a gateway restart.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing.

The Blockchain/Testing entry points aim at the slot that was named — KS-1382

What to know. The audit, ZAP, tenant-isolation and Schemathesis wrappers used to fall back to slot 1’s gateway when TARGET_BASE was not passed, so a run on a slot-N worktree could attack another session’s stack. A local run now names its slot: SECUURA_STACK_SLOT=2 ./run-internal-audit.sh (or STACK_SLOT). With neither a slot nor TARGET_BASE it refuses with rc 2 and prints the command to run.
TARGET_BASE with no slot is used unchanged (CI, remote targets). With a slot named, a local TARGET_BASE that is another slot’s gateway is refused; a remote host, or a local port that is no slot’s gateway, is left alone.
SECUURA_TARGET_RESOLVE_ONLY=1 prints the resolved target and exits 0 without scanning. The ports come from stack_env.sh; check-stack-safety.sh section 3d fails any typed slot port under Blockchain/Testing.
Not measured here. The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6), a live stack, pre-push legs 3, 4 and 8 (they skip without the stack) and Node 24. No scan was run: the cells prove which URL each script would aim at and which combinations it refuses.
Blockchain/Dev/deployment/azure/RUNBOOK.md line 56 runs ./run-internal-audit.sh with no TARGET_BASE and will now refuse; it is not changed here.

The api-gateway front door answers 503 when an API key cannot be checked; every hand-built gateway fetch is bounded — KS-1240

What to know. A presented sk_ key whose check could not complete (no complete answer from the security service within 5 s, an unreadable body, or a 5xx, 408 or 429, including its own 503 'Unable to verify key') now answers 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE on required and optional mounts and is never cached. It used to answer 401 Invalid API key, and the security service’s 503 was negative-cached for 30 s. A rejected key keeps every answer it had (401 plus 30 s cache; the KS-1207 fall-through on optional mounts).
The connector exchange is bounded at 5 s and stays fail-closed: no Bearer is forwarded and a Bearer-only upstream answers 401, as before. Nine hand-built forwards are bounded at 60 s, the generic proxy’s bound; a body cut off by the timeout is the route’s own 502 instead of the upstream’s status with {}; a batch item whose body never arrived fails instead of succeeding empty; generate-package reads the package before writing the zip headers.
Authority for the 503: Kam’s rulings on cards secuura-ks888-revoke-validate-on-failed-save (a) and secuura-ks1370-validate-when-the-revoke-check-cannot-read (a).
Not measured here. Node 24 (host Node 26.8.1 only, no Docker in this lane); a real security or auth service (the upstreams are stubs); any running stack.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing. The change is not deployed.

A social sign-in provider that does not answer in time is classified as unavailable; every provider fetch is bounded — KS-1240

What to know. The six social provider adapters (GitHub, Google, LinkedIn, Apple, Facebook, Microsoft) bound each of their 12 fetches at 10 s. A call that does not finish in time (a hang, or a body that stalls) surfaces as the existing ServiceUnavailableError: POST /social/link answers 503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, and the login callback answers its 400 “OAuth authentication failed: <Provider> did not answer in time; please try again.” Both used to hang.
A refused or unresolvable provider is classified the same way (“<Provider> could not be reached; please try again.”: 503 on the link route, was 500). A token refusal no longer carries the provider’s own error body to the client: the error says “<Provider> token exchange failed (HTTP 400)” and the body is logged server-side, first 200 characters. The OAuth state is still spent before the exchange, so a timed-out sign-in needs a fresh one.
routes/auth.ts and middleware/errorHandler.ts are unchanged; the classification is thrown from the adapters.
Not measured here. Node 24 (host Node 26.8.1 only, no Docker in this lane); any real provider (the endpoints are a local stub, the state store an in-memory map); any running stack.
The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are not claimed as passing. The change is not deployed.

Tenant creation bounds its two outbound fetches; a hung service ends in the fail-soft path an unreachable one already took — KS-1240 part 4

What to know. POST /api/tenants called the tokenisation service (key generation) and the security service (metering key) with no timeout. Each call now passes AbortSignal.timeout with a 3 s bound (the gateway gives up on this route at 8 s and the two calls run one after the other, so with both services hung the create takes about 6 s plus the route’s own writes). On expiry it fails soft exactly as an unreachable service already did: 201, tenant active, keyFile: null (or meteringApiKey: null), and vault_key_hash stored as NULL when key generation failed.
Only the bounds change. Before, a hung tokenisation service left the request open with the tenant row stuck at provisioning and nothing else written; a hung security service left an active tenant and no answer. What the route should answer when key generation cannot complete is a separate decision: it was decided on 2026-10-28 (option b, an explicit key-pending flag) and is built as a separate PR after this one. A service that answers, but later than the bound, also ends in the fail-soft path: 201 with keyFile (or meteringApiKey) null and vault_key_hash stored as NULL, where the unbounded code waited, returned the key and stored the hash; nothing reads either value.
Scope. The ticket text names only the api-gateway key-validate fetch; this part was added by the coordinator’s brief, not by the ticket.
Not measured here. A real tokenisation or security service, a real database row (the writes are read at a recording stub), a running stack, and the 409 a retry would meet after an unbounded hang (read from the code). The four platform suites (Schemathesis, Akto, Playwright, Performance/k6) are UNMEASURED and not claimed as a pass. The change is not deployed.
Node v26.8.1, not the repository’s stated LTS line of Node 24 (none is installed on this host). The figures are declared as measured on v26.8.1, not claimed as LTS.

What to know. Test-only. Before this change the cell sent subjectEmail and reason (refused with 400) and read the new id from id, requestId or data.id (the route returns it at request.id), so it either failed at the create step or, with a body the route accepted, returned without testing anything. Two originate catches survive a hostile thrown value: the system-errors list and extractPgCode — KS-1780

/api/oauth/authorize: a scope that is not a string is 400 invalid_request — KS-1219

What to know. Behaviour change: GET and POST /api/oauth/authorize now refuse a scope that is present and is not a string with 400 invalid_request. An array (a repeated ?scope= or a JSON array) used to be a 500 server_error; a JSON null, 0 or false used to be read as an absent scope (the default allow-list) and is now a 400. No published message changes.
Wednesday accepted the tightening on 2026-10-28. The cells cover the array carriers and the null, 0 and false edge. Authentication surface. A local model wrote the patch; it is applied unchanged.
Not measured here. A running stack, real clients, tokens or a database, and a Node 24 LTS run (the host is Node 26.8.1). The other authorize parameters are not covered: a repeated state or code_challenge on GET still answers 500, and scope is neither deduplicated nor bounded (separate ticket, KS 1797).

services/auth: a weak-password 422 now carries its feedback in the published array shape (AppError prototype fix) — KS-1467

What to know. Behaviour change: the registration, password-reset and change-password routes answer a weak password with a 422 that now carries error.details as an array, one { code, path, message } per feedback line, which is the shape the published ValidationErrorResponse already promises (round 1 sent { feedback: [...] }; the gate caught it and round 2 conforms). The cause was the services/auth AppError constructor resetting every subclass to the base prototype, so the error handler’s instanceof ValidationError branch never ran; it now uses new.target.prototype. The route for a role not permitted on /register has no details and is unchanged.
The feedback is fixed strings from checkPasswordStrength and cannot echo the candidate password; cell N1 and the three route E cells prove that and fail if a route is made to echo (nine mutants, three per route, each turns red only its own route’s cells). The packages/shared half is separate. A local model wrote the first patch; it is applied unchanged.
Not measured here. Whether the front end shows the details, a running stack (the routes ran with the repository and database replaced), and a Node 24 LTS run (the host is Node 26.8.1).

Akto 2.42.6, and the pinned versions are the minimum versions — KS-1609

What to know. Akto pin 2.42.6 (both 2.42.6_local tags, 2026-10-30; dashboard-only 2.42.3 and 2.42.5 skipped). akto-puppeteer-replay 1.51.3; mongo:7.0.45 unchanged. oxlint 1.90.0 / oxlint-tsgolint 7.0.2005 in all five systemTest packages that pin them, and @oxlint/plugins 1.90.0; observability moved to oxlint 1.90.0 too. v2.42.7 exists for the dashboard only (testing has no tag yet), so 2.42.6 is the latest pair.
Minimum versions. npm run start refuses an AKTO_IMAGE_VERSION, AKTO_DASHBOARD_VERSION or AKTO_TESTING_VERSION below the pin (shell or .env); a newer one is allowed. Every npm range’s floor is the installed version.
Login refused before trying? The harness counts its Akto dashboard logins per slot. When 10 have been made in Akto’s current hour (Akto refills all 10 at once on each full hour from the first login after the dashboard starts), it refuses at once, names when Akto refills and docker restart <dashboard>, which resets Akto’s limiter. npm run update-images now reads five pages of tags and names package.json as the pin.
Review .txt. An Akto run’s runs/<hexId>.txt now states the verdict, with Akto’s raw count on a labelled line.
Catalog STALE? The tiers and npm run start now stop on it. Run npm run generate-test-ids && npm run format, read the id diff (a new id is a new Akto test, a missing one is coverage Akto removed), and commit.

A backup code can no longer be spent twice by two sign-ins at once — KS-1848, KS-1849

What to know. The MFA backup-code burn in services/auth is now one conditional UPDATE (userRepo.consumeBackupCode): it removes exactly the stored hash that matched, and only while it is still stored. Before, the gate wrote its own snapshot of the array, minus the code, back over the row by id.
Behaviour change on POST /api/auth/login, the OAuth password grant and wallet verify: of two sign-ins racing with one backup code, the second now gets the uniform refusal (the answer a replayed spent code gets) and one lockout count, instead of a second credential. A burn that cannot be confirmed (a blind write, a NULL set, no row) still answers 503 with the KS-1188 F3 wording. The burn still comes before the credential.
New cells: ks1848-backup-code-burn-is-conditional.test.ts (18 unit cells over a stateful db stub; 9 are red at develop) and integration/ks1848-backup-code-burn-concurrency.integration.ts (13 cells on a real Postgres, run as secuura_app so RLS is live; 8 are red at develop). The two ks781 files gain the new function in their userRepo mock.

Docs fragments: each PR adds two small files and one step builds the two pages — KS-1887

What to know. Do not edit the two pages. Add Projects Documents/fragments/flow/KS-NNNN-slug.html (it starts <h2><!--n-->. Title (KS-NNNN)</h2>; never type a section number, and write <!--n-->.1 in a sub-heading) and Projects Documents/fragments/cheat/KS-NNNN-slug.html (a plain entry, no tokens). One <h2> per file; the name is the ticket key plus a lowercase slug, unique in its directory; a PR with two entries adds two files per page.
Before you push run node Blockchain/Dev/scripts/docs-fragments.mjs check; it must end in OK, and the line goes in the PR’s Test Evidence. Leg 14 runs the same check for any push that changes Blockchain/Dev; a push that touches only Projects Documents/ skips the gate, so run it yourself.
To read the result: build --out "$TMPDIR/preview" writes both pages to a scratch directory. A section number is provisional until the PR merges (it follows merge order). Whoever merges runs the refresh after a batch of merges (build --base <ls-remote develop sha>, commit just the two pages), so the committed pages may lag; check says by how many entries.
A PR that still carries a hand-written entry brings it across once with adopt --from-ref "$OLD" --against "$D" after merging develop in and taking develop’s two pages (the commands are in SKILL.md section 4). A page edited by hand is only an advisory: the next refresh runs adopt first.
Not measured here. The four platform suites, a running stack, Node 24 and any deploy were not run. Two stacked PRs converting the same base entry were not tested together. A PR that edits existing page text (#1383) needs a hand step.

Running the cells

KS-1384 F-a: row counts, payloadTimestamp, originate stops writing anchor_store, and the reconciler’s give-up

What to know.Anchoring’s status UPDATE can expect a status (or a list) and a hash (or no hash), and the runner says how many rows matched, or null when the write did not run. The reconciler reads it; the submitter does not yet.
POST /api/anchors answers payloadTimestamp (the stored payload’s anchoring time; the kept payload’s time when a repeat request did not replace it), and GET /api/anchors/:id answers it too. Additive.
originate’s POST /api/anchoring/anchor and /submit no longer write anchor_store; anchoring persists its own row.
The reconciler fails a row only 24 h after its last write and after two consecutive passes in which the chain answered “not found”; a provider outage never fails a row. Each of its writes expects the status and hash it read.
Not measured here.A real provider outage, the four platform suites, a running stack, Node 24 (LTS), any box.

Billing customer routes act only for the customer’s owner or a platform administrator — KS-1888

What to know. Twelve billing routes that take a customer, invoice or payment-method id, plus POST /customers, now act only for the customer’s owner (metadata.userId equals the caller’s userId, and the tenant where one is recorded) or a platform administrator. Anyone else gets the same 404 as an unknown id. PUT /customers/:id refuses the certificationCredits, userId and tenantId metadata keys with 400. GET /customers/by-email now runs at all.
A test or tool that calls these routes needs a caller who owns the customer, or a platform administrator. Its Stripe double needs findCustomer (and getPaymentMethod or listCustomersByEmail where the route uses them).
A customer created before this change carries whatever userId its creator posted, and the check trusts it. That is a limit of the fix, not a finding: there is no other owner record to check against.
Not measured here. A running stack; Stripe; callers outside this repository; how many existing customers carry a wrong userId; the four platform suites; Node 24; any deploy.

Running the cells

mcp-server hash tool keeps its isError result when the thrown value is unreadable — KS-1868, mcp-server

What to know. The catch in the secuura_hash_document tool read the thrown value unguarded; a revoked Proxy or a throwing message getter made the read throw inside the catch, so the handler rejected instead of returning isError. The read is now in an inner try with the fallback 'unreadable thrown value'. 3 new cells in 1 file; the suite is 7 files / 33 cells, all pass.
One held Spark pass applied strictly; no hand edit. The returned error text is not changed: it is still the raw message of whatever hashFile threw (KS 1893).
Not measured here. KS-1868 stays open (one of its 253 sites). The four platform suites, a running stack, an MCP client, an LTS Node, npm ci on the merged lockfile and any deploy were not run.
